MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-01-09 | CLIP_ID: 567 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 567

  RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
 Office of City Clerk
January 9, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS
- Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl,
City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.).  Mr. Luna moved to approve the Consent Agenda, seconded by
Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 27, 2016.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from “Sine Die” Session of the Governing Body on January 3, 2017.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from the First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 3, 2017.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Johnson moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. White.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input to determine whe ther
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exists for Tract 71 and
portions of Tracts 70 & 72, Allison Tracts, 2nd Filing and adjacent right of way, being situated in
a portion of the SW¼, NW¼, and the NW¼, SW¼ of Section 8, T13N, R66W of the 6  Principalth
Meridian in Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of W.
Allison Road and Walter scheid Blvd.).  E ric Ha mmer, De velopment Depa rtment, provided
information on the property requested by the landowners to be annexed and reviewed requirements
of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Bonnie Reider, adjacent
property owner, read a message she had sent to the governing body expressing her concerns with the
proposed annexation and affiliated zone change ordinance which would involve annexing only a
portion of Waltersche id Blvd. and West Allison R oad, which w ill affect her prope rty; creating
county pockets; cur b, g utter and sidew alk maintenanc e, and e mergency responde rs and law
enforcement jurisdiction.  Ms. Reider requested consideration to either include all of Walterscheid
Blvd. or exclude Walterscheid and West Allison Road from the annexation area, and to postpone
action on the item to obtain more in formation on annexation impact.  Curt Buchhammer, general
counsel for South Cheyenne Water and Sewer (SCWS) District, expressed his opinion that not all
statutory requirements had been met relative to certified mailing notification to landowners within
the territory (i.e. the District) and protection of health, safety and welfare of persons residing in the
area to be annexed.  He questioned what purpose the annexation would serve; noted services already
being provided to the prope rty by the District and t hose involving County  and City  emergency
response and law enforcement services; commented that additional investigation and research is
needed, and requested postponement.  Ben Marszalek, Vice President, SCWS District, referenced
the 1970 ag reement between the City and the District a nd expressed his opinion that there wa s
nothing in the agreement that provided for the City to annex only a portion of the D istrict.  Brad
Emmons, AVI, agent for the applicant, provided a brief background on purpose of the applicant
requesting annexation and  replatting of the property, which is to address a legal property trust issue.
Mr. Emmons noted they had met recently with the District and the District had recommended that
the summary  of anne xation repor t (i.e. und er Annexation Report Data ( iii) contained in the
Annexation Report and Staff Report supporting documentation) include wording that extension of
services initiated by the property owner would be to the SCWS District, which would clarify that if
the property is annexed applicable services would be through the SCWD District and not the City’s
Board of Public Utilities.  Mayor Orr noted for those present that there would be no action taken on
the proposed annexation at the conclusion of the public hearing as the purpose of the hearing is to
allow the opportunity  for public c omment.  She further  noted Ag enda Items #13 and #14 ar e
ordinances related to the public hearing and would not be discussed or acted upon at the meeting as
they are  on first rea ding a nd would be re ferred to P ublic Service s Commi ttee for  revie w and
recommendation, including a dditional opportunity for public c omment.  May or Orr advised the
Committee will meet January 18, 2017 at noon in Room #122, Municipal Building.  There being no
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the public
hearing.

 RESOLUTION - To name the building complex located at 1328 Talbot Court in the City of

[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2017 budget of the City of Cheyenne

[CA] Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Saddlehorn Pipeline Company, LLC, to

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6613)

Remediation Phase 2 Projec t (County Consensus Block G rant).  Motion (pe r Consent Ag enda)

Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King Holiday on Monday, January
16, 2017, reg ularly schedule d City  Council Comm ittee meeting s will inst ead be  held in the
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance
Committee will meet Tuesday, January 17, 2017 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will

Under other business, Mr. Johnson congratulated the local Sean Curtis Band for receiving
awards at the 2016 Rocky Mountain Country Music Awards; thanked individuals providing snow
removal services to those residents who are unable to do so and those who have donated to several

MEETING: Sine Die Session and First Regular Meeting | DATE: 2017-01-03 | CLIP_ID: 569 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 569

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 3, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” Session on this date at 6:00
p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Those present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Jim Brown, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal,
Dicky Shanor, Jeff White and Annette Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk.  The
pledge of allegiance, led by members of the Mayor’s Youth Council, was recited.
Mayor Ka ysen welcomed those in attenda nce and stated the purpose  of the “ Sine Die”
session was administrative in nature to ceremonially recognize outgoing Council members for their
years of service on City Council, as well as the outgoing City Council President for 2016.
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read a ceremonial resolution in its
entirety ho noring ou tgoing Cou ncil m ember Jim B rown, e ntitled a s f ollows:  “ A R esolution in
Recognition of the Honorable Jim Brown for his dedication and outstanding contributions to the City
of Cheyenne and its residents.”  Mayor Kaysen presented Mr. Brown with a framed version of the
Resolution and expressed appreciation to Mr. Brown for his 10 years of service on City Council and
to the community. Mr. Brown briefly commented on his terms of service as a City Council member.
(#5822)
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Ms. Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring
outgoing Council member Annette Williams, entitled as follows:  “A Resolution in recognition of the
Honorable Annette Williams for her dedication and outstanding contributions to the City of Cheyenne
and its residents.”  Mayor Kaysen presented Ms. Williams with a framed version of the Resolution and
expressed appr eciation to Ms. W illiams for her 4 y ears of servic e on City  Council and to the
community.  Ms. Williams briefly commented on he r term of  service as a City  Council member.
(#5823)
Mayor Kaysen relinquished duties of the cha ir to City Council President Bryan Cook.  Mr.
Cook requested Ms. Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring Mr. Kaysen as
outgoing Mayor, entitled as follows: “A Resolution in recognition of the Honorable Richard L. Kaysen
for his dedication and outstanding contributions to the City of Cheyenne and its residents.”  Mr. Cook
presented Mr. Kaysen with a framed version of the Resolution and also presented him with a gavel
plaque depicting his 8 years of service as Mayor.  Mayor Kaysen briefly commented on his 2 terms as
Mayor. (#5824)
Mr. Cook then returned duties of the chair to Mayor Kaysen.  On behalf of the governing body,
Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation to Mr. Cook for his leadership as City Council President for
2016 and presented him with an engraved gavel plaque.  Mr. Cook briefly commented on his service
as City Council President for the past year.
Mayor Kaysen announced the First Regular Meeting of the governing body involving newly
elected and continuing members would convene after a musical performance by The Squash Blossoms.
There being no further business previously noted for the “Sine Die” Session and purpose of
the meeting having been met, the meeting was officially adjourned at 6:23 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
PUBLISH: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
January 9, 2017
   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 3, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its First Regular Meeting for
2017 on this date at the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Prior to the meeting  being officially convened,
Dave Lerner presided as Master of Ceremonies for pre-meeting activities which began at 6:30 p.m..
It was noted purpose  of the F irst Reg ular Mee ting wa s administrative in nature.  D uring the
activities, posting of colors was conducted by members of the Cheyenne Police Department and
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue D epartment Honor Gua rd; an invoca tion was provided by  the Ver y
Reverend Carl Gallinger, Diocesan Administrator, Diocese of Cheyenne, and the Honorable Matt
Mead, Governor of the State of Wyoming, presented a Governor’s address.  The Honorable Justice
William U. Hill, Wyoming Supreme Court, administered the oath of office to Mayor Marian J. Orr
and to the following newly elected and re-elected members of City Council: Pete Laybourn and Jeff
White, Ward I; Bryan M. Cook and Dicky  Shanor, Ward II, and Rocky S. Case and Mike L una,
Ward III.  Welcoming comments were made Mayor Orr.
Mayor Orr officially called the First Regular Meeting of the governing body to order at 7:03
p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Those present were: MAYOR - Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, P ete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff  White.  Also present was Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk.
The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Approval of appointment of City  Officials (pursuant to Title 2 of the Code of the  City of
Cheyenne).  Mayor Orr stated that, pursuant to state law, as these are Mayoral appointees, she would
not be participating in d iscussion or voting on the proposed appointments.  M r. Luna moved to
approve the appointee s “en ma sse”, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Johnson moved to divide the
question to allow for a separate vote on each of the proposed appointees, seconded by Mr. Roybal.
Motion failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” --
Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  M ain motion carried with all
members of the governing body voting “yes” with the exception of Mr. J ohnson voting “no” and
Mayor Orr not casting a vote.
Election of City Council President for 2017.  Mayor Orr stated that, pursuant to City Code,
the mayor conducts the elec tion for Council Pr esident but does not cast a vote .  Mr. L aybourn
moved to nominate and elect Mr. Shanor as City Council President for 2017, seconded by Mr. Luna.
Mr. Shanor accepted the nomination.  Mr. Luna moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr.
Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mayor Orr who did not vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Shanor as City Council President
carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting
“no” and Mayor Orr who did not vote.
Election of City Council Vice President for 2017.  Mayor Orr stated that, pursuant to City
Code, the mayor conducts the election for Council Vice-President but does not ca st a vote.  Mr.
Shanor moved to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council Vice President for 2017, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Dr. Rinne accepted the nomination.  Mr. White moved to nominate and e lect Mr.
Johnson as City Council Vice President for 2017, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Johnson accepted
the nomination.  Mr. Shanor moved that nominations cea se, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who
did not vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council Vice President for 2017 car
ried.  Voting “yes”-- Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” --
Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Mayor Orr did not vote.
Following the benediction by the Very Reverend Carl Gallinger, Mayor Orr announced light
refreshments would be served in the Civic Center lobby following adjournment of the meeting, and
extended appre ciation to those involved in the prog ram a ctivities and City  staff, or ganization
members and individuals who assisted with arrangements for the evening’s meetings. 
There being no further business previously noted for the First Regular Meeting and purpose
of the meeting have been met, the meeting was officially adjourned at 7:17 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
January 9, 2017

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-01-23 | CLIP_ID: 572 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 572

  RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
 Office of City Clerk
January 23, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS
- Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl,
City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.).  Mr. Cook moved to approve the Consent Agenda, seconded by
Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 9, 2017.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Tyrrell
Chevrolet) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as
presented, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to
Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE – 2  READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 (b) of the Cheyennend
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 2.1.1 for the purpose of creating a subdivision
plat waiver process for limited lot line adjustments of platted properties.  Re porting for Public
Services Committee, Mr. Ro ybal moved to approve  on second r eading, seconded by Mr. Case.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE – 2  READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Tract 71 andnd
portions of Tracts 70 & 72, Allison Tracts, 2  Filing and adjacent right of way, being situated innd
portions of the SW¼, NW ¼, and the NW¼, SW¼, of Section 8, T13N, R66W of the 6  Principalth
Meridian in Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of W.
Allison Road and Waltersheid Blvd.).  Bonnie Reider, adjacent property owner, spoke in opposition
to the proposed annexation, noting that, if the ordinance is approved, her property would still be in
the county, and citing concerns with only annexing a portion of Walterscheid Blvd. and
responsibilities she may incur relating to snow removal and curb and gutter maintenance.  M r.
Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Upon inquiry, Brad Emmons,
AVI, agent for the applicant, provided information on general policy regarding annexation of roads
to connect with those within t he City closest to the prope rty to be ann exed, and Eric Ha mmer,
Development Department, verified that a representative from the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer
District (SCWSD) was not in attendance at the meeting, therefore, he was unsure if their concerns
had been resolved.  Discussion included extending Walterscheid Blvd. to South Greeley Highway
and comments made by the attorney for the SCWSD at the public hearing.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
ORDINANCE – 2  READING – Pu rsuant to Sections 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,nd
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MR – Medium Density Residential to
MR-2 – Medium Density Residential – Developing for Tract 71 and portions of Tracts 70 & 72,
Allison Tracts, 2nd Filing located in the SW¼, of Section 8, T13N, R66W of the 6  Principalth
Meridian in Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located near the intersection of W. Allison Road
and Walterscheid Boulevard) to be known as Rorabaugh Subdivision.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body.
ORDINANCE – 2  READING – Pu rsuant to Sections 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,nd
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified
Development Code , changing the zoning classification f rom LR-1 Low Density R esidential--
Established to NR-1 Neighborhood Residential -- Moderate Density for Lot 1, Block 5, Eastridge
Third Filing subdivision, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at 1800 Milton
Drive).  Mayor Orr advised the a pplicant had submitted a re quest that the ordinance, as we ll as
affiliated Agenda Item #13 (Resolution for Final Plat for Eastridge Fifth Filing) be withdrawn from
the agenda.  U pon inquiry to Cit y Council members and no objection being  raised, Ma yor Orr
announced the ordinance and affiliated resolution were being withdrawn from the agenda.
ORDINANCE – 1  READING – Amending the Major Street Plan Official Map 2015, byst
adding the following roadway segments: Storey Boulevard extension from Highland Drive to Beckle
Road and Van Buren Avenue extension from Opal Drive to Storey Boulevard – referred to Public
Services Committee.  (Sponsor - Mr. Roybal)
ORDINANCE –  1  REA DING – A mending Se ction 5 .36.030, Public A ddresses,st
Entertainment and Advertising on Streets, of Chapter 5.36, Circuse s, Carnivals and Amuse ment
Rides, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming
– referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsor - Mr. Johnson)
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Eastridge
Fifth Filing, a replat of Lot 1 Block 5 of ‘Eastridge Third Filing ’located at 1800 Milton Drive, City
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southeast cor ner of Milton Dri ve and
Durham Road). [Clerk’s note:  As previously noted earlier in the meeting, the item was withdrawn
from the agenda.]
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for South
Ridge Addition, a replat of Lot 2, Block 1 and a 20’ wide alley of Estevan’s Subdivision, situate
within a portion of the SW¼ N W¼ Section 34, T.14N., R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Laramieth
County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Randy Road, west of Ridg e Road).   Patr ice
Gapen, property owner in the vicinity, spoke in opposition to the resolution citing concerns with the
proposed low income housing project; another low income housing project planned for the area, and
drainage, parking, traffic control and single point of access.  Ms. Gapen encouraged either denial or
postponement of the resolution.  Bill Fehringer, Civil Engineering Professionals, Inc., agent for the
applicant, provided information regarding the Final Plat, which will divide the lot into four parcels;
Wyoming Community Development Authority’s review and approval for the project; setbacks, oil
pipeline located on an adjacent property; proposed detention pond, and access availability of Fire
Department response vehicles to the site.  He advised a site plan had been submitted to the City on
this date.  Mr. Roy bal moved to adopt with staff  recommendations #1 and 2, sec onded by Mr.
Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Fehringer explained the detention pond would be located west of the
existing chiropractor and restaurant businesses and provided information on proposed height of the
buildings.  Discus sion in cluded th e de tention po nd; he ight o f b uildings; c oncern wi th t raffic
congestion and single access point, and comment that the site plan will hopefully contain remedies
for c oncerns expressed.  Upon re quest, Carol I ntlekofer, City  Cl erk, read into the re cord the
following two staff recommendations contained within the motion: Add a block number to the Final
Plat map and add “Cheyenne” in the appropriate location of the title block on t he Final Plat map.
Seth Lloyd, Development Department, clarified that site plans do not come before City Council for
approval.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr.
White.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.  Mayor Orr confirmed she
did not cast a vote. [Clerk’s note: Following adjournment of the meeting and pursuant to requirement
of City Code, which requires a majority affirmative vote of governing body members present for the
item to pass (i.e. six “yes” votes), it was determined the resolution failed].

had been met.  M r. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  K evin Georges, Laramie
County Liquor Association, provided a brief history involving the annual request.  Carol Intlekofer,
City Clerk, clarified the dates of the four requested Sundays were: February 5  (Super Bowl Sunday),th
July 23  (first weekend of Frontier Days), September 3  (Labor Day weekend) and December 31rd rd st
(New Year’s Eve).  Comment was made regarding City ordinance restriction of Sunday operating
hours.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5828)

(1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
Under other business:
Mr. J ohnson cong ratulated those  who participated in the rec ent B allet Wy oming’s
performance of Peter Pan; thanked the community for donated items toward several non-profit
projects in which he is involved, and noted new and on-going community based projects currently
in progress. 
Upon inquiry by Mr. White, Mayor Orr provided an update on the former Police Department
building with regard to process being discussed to dispose of the building, which will include public
participation opportunity.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 6:48 p.m.

February 13, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS
- Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor
and Jeff White.  Absent: Richard Johnson.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia
Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.).  Mr. Shanor declared he would abstain from participation on the
item due to a business relationship involving the State Loan and Investment Board (Consent Agenda
Item #18), and vacated Council Chambers.  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support
received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Cook, Agenda Item #28 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.

-- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers.

body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to
Council Chambers.

ORDINANCE – 2  READING – Amending the Major Street Plan Official Map 2015, bynd
adding the following roadway segments: Storey Boulevard extension from Highland Drive to Beckle

to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members

ORDINANCE –  2  R EADING –  A mending S ection 5 .36.030, P ublic A ddresses,nd
Entertainment and Advertising on Stree ts, of Chapter 5.36, Circuse s, Carnivals and Amuse ment
Rides, of Title 5, Business L icenses and Regu lations, of the Code of  the City  of Chey enne,
Wyoming.  Anthony Vibbard spoke in favor of the ordinance and referenced a newspaper article he
had written recently on the topic, and commented on constitutional rights.  Reporting for Finance
Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Shanor
moved to amend the last sentence  under Sec tion 5.36.030 by  adding the word “ written” so the

of the owner, manager or occupant of the premises in front of or a djoining the place such person
proposes to give such public address, entertainment or advertising”.  Discussion included the types
of scenarios that may fall under the ordinance; difficulty in obtaining written consent from property
owners and when consent would not be required; law enforcement, and encouraging “busking” and
street performances in the public rig hts-of-way in the downtown ar ea.  Motion to amend f ailed.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” – Mayor Orr, Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, February 27, 2017 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal applications
for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and
microbrewery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, February 27, 2017 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a retail liquor license
transfer of owners hip application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Lincolnway, Inc. d/b/a
Lincolnway Liquor, 2825 East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Mason
Properties, LLC dba Lincolnway Liquor, 2825 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY).
Announcement was made that due to the Presidents’ Holiday on Monday, February 20, 2017,
regularly schedule d City  Council Committee mee tings will instead be he ld in the Municipal
Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday , February 21, 2017 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Service s Committee will meet
Wednesday, February 22, 2017 at Noon.
Under other business:
Dr. Rinne stated that at the last City Council meeting on January 23, 2017 the vote regarding
adoption of the resolution authorizing a Final Plat for South Ridge Addition was inadvertently
reported as approved when in fact not enough affirmative votes were received to achieve passage.
As he voted on the prevailing side, Dr. Rinne moved to reconsider the resolution to allow the
applicant an opportunity to provide additional information relating to the proposed Final Plat, and
to provide a clear record of the final action that will be taken on the resolution, seconded by Mr.
Shanor.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the following entitlement of the item to be reconsidered
into the record: Resolution - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for South
Ridge Addition, a replat of Lot 2, Block 1 and a 20' wide alley of Estevan’s Subdivision, situate
within a portion of the SW1/4 NW1/4 Section 34, T.14N., R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Laramieth
County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Randy Road, west of Ridge Road).  Randy Reed
with the law firm of Dray, Dyekman, Reed and Healey, representing South Ridge apartment group
owners, commented that all plat requirements have been met, and introduced others present at the
meeting who wo uld b e p roviding additional i nformation o n t he p roposed p roject.  S am Long,
Summit Housing Group, project owner, provided a brief history and current status of  the Group’s
affordable housing pr oject at the site, inc luding a pproval a nd financ ing involving the WCDA
(Wyoming Community Development Association), other projects the Group has been involved in
or have planned, zoning and density level, and the need for affordable housing in the community.
During his comments, Mr. Long provided a hand-out (on file with City Clerk’s office) reflecting a
rendering of proposed building designs.  Jen Clary, Encompass v2, architectural consultant for the
project, also provided information about the project, noting they have not requested any variances.
Ms. Clary commented on dr ainage control, fire access, parking and building height.  During her
comments, Ms. Clary  submitt ed a ha nd-out (on file w ith Ci ty Clerk’s off ice) reflecting  a 3-
dimensional drawing of the height of the proposed buildings with slope and grading plans.  Patrice
Gapen spoke in opposition to the project, stating concerns with density, whether housing of this type
was needed pursuant to information provided by the Cheyenne Housing Authority (CHA), setbacks,
height of buildings, size of the site footprint vs. the project, drainage, parking, access and traffic
congestion, and other projects currently underway for similar affordable housing units in the area.
Mr. Long advised that WCDA had conducted a site visit last year; that CHA had provided a letter
concerning the waiting list for affordable housing, that a market study reflected a high need for this
type of housing, and that the project meets UDC (Uniform Development Code) compliance.  Upon
inquiry from Patti Riesland, May or’s Council for People  with Disabiliti es, Ms. Clary  stated 1
dwelling in each unit will be a Type A under the ADA with a parking space provided.  Discussion
included location of Ms. Gapen’s property in the vicinity, which consists of 4-plex apartment units;
project meeting compliance with City requirements; shortage of affordable housing and vacancy
rates.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with
the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”.  Main motion to adopt the resolution carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Luna and Mayor Orr voting
“no”. (#5834)
There being no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m. 
 S ubmitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-02-27 | CLIP_ID: 584 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 584

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 Office of City Clerk 
 
February 27, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Marian J. Orr; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS - Rocky Case, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent: Bryan Cook.  Also present: Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of  [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no 
separate dis cussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and 
support by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be 
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.). Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and purs uant to 
support received by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Johnson, Agenda Item #12 was removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. 
Roybal and Mr. Shanor, Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- 
all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 13, 2017.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Tyrrell 
Chevrolet) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as 
presented, seconded by Mr. Johnson.   Motion carried.  Voting “ye s” -- all members of the 
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal 
returned to Council Chambers. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for renewal 
applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill 
liquor licenses and microbrewery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  City Clerk Carol 
Intlekofer noted that information contained in the bar and grill renewal liquor licen se application 
filed by S & S Wyoming, LLC d/b/a The Suite Bistro, 1901 Central Avenue, reflected non -
compliance with state statute renewal criteria.  She stated that, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 12 -4-
408, the renewal application must reflect that not less than 60% of gross sales from the preceding 
12 months of operation be derived from food services and not alcoholic and malt beverage sales.  
The Suite Bistro disclosed on its renewal application that approximately 37% of its gross sales 
was from food sales an d 63% was from liquor sales.  Ms. Intlekofer clarified the renewal 
application was not included in those annual licenses involved with the public hearing, and the 
application would also not be included in the renewal licenses that will be considered for 
approval at the next F inance Committee and City Council  meetings.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the 
hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened  a public hearing to receive comments and input for a retail liquor 
license transfer of ownership application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Lincolnway, Inc. 
d/b/a Lincolnway Liquor, 2825 East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held 
by Mason Properties, LLC dba Lincolnway Liquor, 2825 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY).  John 
Mason, applicant and member of Mason Properties, LLC and Lincolnway, Inc. explained the 
purpose of requesting the transfer, which is to separate the business operating entity from the real 
property owner entity.  There being  no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Major Street Plan Official Map 2015, by 
adding the following roadway segments: Storey Boulevard extension from Highland Drive to 
Beckle Road and Van Buren Avenue extension from Opal Drive to Storey Boulevard.  Joe 
Patterson, Guardian Development Company and Hom es by Guardian, explained they have been 
working on a master plan for a project involving this area and commented  on anticipated road 
design and connectivity which may not line up with the proposed official map.  He requested the 
item be postponed or recognition be provided that in the future their proposed road design will be 
considered.  Tom Mason, Director, Metropolitan Planning Organization, explained this is a 
center line survey for the extension and when the developer comes forward with a plat plan, t hat 
consideration could be given that alignment of the roadways could be changed as long as it meets 
the intent of the extension.   Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” -- 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
(#4167) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 5.36.030, Public Addresses, 
Entertainment and Advertising on Streets, of Chapter 5.36, Circuses, Carnivals and Amusement 
Rides, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  C oncern was voiced relating to the scenario involving a 
business not having the ability to stop an undesired activity in front of their storefront. Mr. 
Shanor moved to amend the last sentence in Section 5.36.030 to read as follows, seconded by 
Mr. Luna:  Persons shall obtain the consent of the owner or manager of the premises in front of 
the place such person proposes to give such public address, entertainment or advertising.  Brief 
comment followed on business manager’s authority to provide consent.  Moti on carried.  Voting 
“yes” -- Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” -- 
Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Roybal.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
(#4168) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING- Repealing Section 10.52.050, Parking for certain 
purposes prohibited, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the  City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Georgia Broyles spoke in 
opposition to the ordinance and commented on the initial ordinance serving a positive purpose in 
the community and how repealing it will affect law enforcement and the Fight the Blight task 
force.  Chie f Brian Kozak, Cheyenne Police Department, advised that upon inquiry to Police 
Community Service Officers (CSO’s) he has learned that CSO’s use this City Code section to 
“green tag” vehicles park ed illegally on the streets but have not issued official cita tions.  Mr. 
Roybal reported there was no recommendation on this item from Public Services Committee.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, 
Chief Kozak provided further information involving “tagging” vehicl es that have a for sale sign 
in the window and that the “tag” is considered a written warning.  Discussion topics included: 
Wise water conservation program relative to washing vehicles in the street; conducting non -
emergency vehicle mechanical repairs; oth er sections of City Code that may apply for certain 
violations; nuisance laws and original City areas being more affected by the current restriction 
than newer subdivisions.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Mr. Case who voted “yes”. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING - Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending various sections of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC) to consolidate the LR -1, LR-2, MR-1, MR-2, HR-1, and HR -2 zone 
districts into LR, MR, a nd HR zone districts.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Eric Hammer, Development office, explained the 
proposed change had been in the works for quite some time and was an item addressed by the 
Critical Path Committee a few years ago as something that could be addressed in the future. He 
provided information on past Planning Commission review and a work session that had been 
scheduled.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Directing the City Clerk to schedule a Public Hearing pursuant to 
Section 5.12.040(2) of the Cheyenne City Code to receive public comment on violations of City 
ordinances and State laws pertaining to the sale of alcoholic beverages, which violations have 
occurred at ‘Peppermill Bar and Liquor’ and to provide a notice of said Public Hearing to 
Minglemill, Inc., DBA ‘Peppermill Bar and Liquor’.  John O’Grady, owner, Peppermill Bar and 
Liquor, explained that steps have been taken to prev ent the issues that led to the violations 
including purchase of scanners to read patrons’ identification, and training for employees.  Mr. 
White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion failed. Voting “no” -  all members of 
the governing body present. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the expedient redevelopment of the building located at 
1602 Capitol Avenue, commonly referred to as the historic  Hynds Building.  David Hatch, 
developer for the Hynds Building project, commented in support of the resolution and distributed 
information (on file with City Clerk’s office) to the governing body pertaining to the project.  He 
explained the history of the project and voiced parking concerns.  He advised that the original 
request for on -street parking  has been removed, and they are requesting 15 spaces  be made 
available in the Spiker Parking Garage in addition to parking in the proposed new area referred 
to as the “Hole”.  Christina Bird, Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board, spoke in support of the 
resolution, and abo ut revitalizing and developing downtown . Jon Jelinek, owner of the 
Paramount Café and Majestic Building, spoke in opposition of the resolution  commenting on  
density, redeveloping and concentration of businesses downtown . Dale Steenbergen, Greater 
Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, spoke in support of the resolution, and about the complexity 
of the project due to  size and financial commitment,  the need for parking, the importance of the 
project for downtown,  investing in historic buildings and economic developmen t. Mr. White 
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by  removing the 
following language from the 10th “Whereas” Clause, “the City is willing to commit up to ten (10) 
designated on street parking spaces adjacent to the perimeter of the Hynds Building” and the 
following language from the “Now Therefore” Clause, Section #2, “up to ten (10) designated on-
street parking spaces adjacent to the perimeter of the Hynds Building and …” seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Mr. Shanor offered a friendly amendment  to the motion on the floor  by adding 10 
additional designated spaces in the parking garage to make a total of 15.  Mr. White, as maker of 
the motion to amend, and  Mr. Case, as second to the motion, concurred with acceptance of the 
friendly amendment . Discussion continued on a substitute resolution  dated 2/27/2017 that 
addressed changes made at the 2/21/2017 Finance Committee meeting, removing the on-street 
parking request and changing the designated spots in the parking garage from 5 to 15.  Motion to 
amend failed . Voting “yes” – Mr. Shanor and Mr. Luna.  Voting “no” - Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn, Mr. Case, Mr. White, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mayor Orr.  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
amend by  substitute resolution  dated 2/27/17 , seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend 
carried. Voting “yes” - all members of the gov erning body present. Discussion continued and 
included: executive summary from the  Attorney’s office regarding constitutionality of the 
resolution; timeline and aggressive schedule  for the project, and plans for the main level of  the 
building.  Mr. Hatch, upon inquiry, provided information on anticipated construction renovation 
plans and potential tenants for the building. Further discussion continued on the estimated cost of 
the project, with $750,000 being the maximum City contribution; return investment; increase of 
property taxes with the project;  opportunity to develop  the “Hole”; commitment out of general 
fund reserves, and partner ing with organizations for projects.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City 
Treasurer, explained there is around $13 million dollars in reserves , of which $8 million is 
required to operate for 60 days, leaving approximately $5 million unencumbered. City Attorney, 
Sylvia Hackl, explained upon inquiry, that the resolution is a statement of intent only , and that 
any City financial appropriation will separately approved.   Anja Bendel, Cheyenne Leads, 
explained upon inquiry , that their intent is to acquire the “ Hole” property that is made up of 2 
parcels, combine them and make the property available for development of a parking lot for the 
Hynds Building.  Discussion continued on Cheyenne Leads’ efforts for providing incentives for 
businesses in the area , and design elements o f the proposed parking lot in the “Hole” . Main 
motion as amended carried. Vot ing “yes” - all members of the governing body  present with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting no. (#5835) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2017 budget of the C ity of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. 16 -4-112 & 16-4-113.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried. 
(#5836) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Re-authorizing the Purchasing Division of the City of Cheyenne to 
proceed on a semiannual basis with the sale of surpl us property pursuant to W.S. § 15 -1-112(a).  
Motion (per consent agenda) carried. (#5837) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Declaring as surplus a City -owned property located at 2020 -2022 
Capitol Avenue, legally described as all of Lots 1 and 2, Block 266, Original City,  together 
improvements thereon, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming; authorizing the Director of 
Public Works to issue public notice requesting redevelopment requests for proposals and to 
proceed with proposed sale of said real property pursuant to Wyo. Stat . §15-1-112.  Motion (per 
consent agenda) carried. (#5838) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat 
for Murray Hill Estates 6 th Filing, a Replat of Tract 51, Murray Hill Estates, 3 rd Filing, and Tract 
2, Murray Hill Estates, 5 th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to 
Fox Ridge Drive, south of Twin Mountain Road).  Motion (per consent agenda) carried. (#5839) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Stating the intent to initiate the reclamation of Crow Creek al ong its 
watershed within the City limits of the City of Cheyenne; authorizing the execution of a 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, the City of Cheyenne Board of 
Public Utilities and the Laramie County Conservation District to develo p and implement the 
Reclamation Project; and appointing an Advisory Committee to participate in the project. Jeff 
Geyer, Laramie County Conservation District, spoke about the project  and in favor of the 
resolution. Bob Mason, Curt Gowdy Chapter of Trout Un limited, spoke in favor of the 
resolution.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion continued on the 
importance of revitalizing Crow Creek, Crow Creek being a flood feature,  funding sources for 
the project, design and  implementation of  the project, developing West Cheyenne  and water 
quality and fishing in Crow Creek. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -  all members of the governing 
body present. (#5840) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, and the 
Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority for the purpose of creating a Brownfields Cleanup 
Revolving Loan Fund and associated Loan Review Committee.  Motion (per consent agenda) 
carried. (#6624) 
 
[CA] Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheye nne and Western Research 
and Development, Ltd. for the FY2017 -18 On-Call Traffic Engineering Services Project. (2011 -
2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per consent agenda) carried (in an amount not t o exceed 
$90,294.00). (#6625) 
 
[CA] Consideration for entering into a single source contract with Waste Management of 
Colorado for the disposal of non -hazardous contaminated waste being generated from the 
Wetlands Pumphouse Project. (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  Motion (per consent 
agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $47,285.00).  (#6626) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Contract between the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning 
Organization and AVI P.C. for the Whitney Road 10% Corridor Plan. (MPO funds). Motion (per 
consent agenda) carried. (#6627) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 Contract No. 6494 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and 
Recreation Department, and Construction Specialists Inc. for partial release of the retainage for 
the Lions Park Restroom Renovation.    Motion (per consent agenda) carried. (#6494) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -8-17 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Pole Mountain Electric, Inc., Cheyenne, for the Dell Range/Yellowstone Signal 
Upgrade Project. (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per consent agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $138,098.58).  (#6628) 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-12-17 for furnishing 160 2-yard front end load containers for the 
Sanitation Division. (Solid Waste Management funds).   Motion (per con sent agenda) carried 
(approved bid submitted by  Wastequip Mfg. Co., LLC, Statesville, NC in an amount not  to 
exceed $59,480.00).  
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -14-17 for furnishing new landfill portable litter fences Phase 2 
for the City’s Landfill Divisi on. (Solid Waste Management funds) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
  
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Johnson provided an update on several community projects in which he is involved. 
  
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the m eeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:42 p.m.   
 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
         
       Carol Intlekofer, MMC   
       City Clerk

Office of City Clerk 
 
March 13, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Pr esent were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike 
Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present:  Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no 
separate discussion on these ite ms unless a member of the governing body so requests and 
support by two other members is received. Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be 
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)   Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to 
support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item #16 (a) was removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Mr. Cook moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

(per Consent Agenda) carried.

February 28, 2017.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
  Mr. Luna moved to appro ve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the Governing Body.

5 
Attorney has advised that this statement on the record provides sufficient documentation to 
identify those individual renewal applications that may be acted upon at the current meeting.  
Upon query by Mayor Orr to City Council members, there was no obje ction voiced to recognize 
the renewal applicants as listed on the agenda in lieu of the Clerk reading the individual 
applicants, and Mayor Orr announced the reading of renewal applicants’ information would be 
waived.  Scott Turner, Marketing Manager, Sam’s  Club, Cheyenne, stated Sam’s Club had been 
notified last week regarding signage at the Club indicating that non -Club members were eligible 
to purchase products from the alcohol dispensing room, and verified the signage had been placed 
in error and that pu rchasers do need to be a Sam’s Club member pursuant to verbal information 
Club representatives had provided the governing body when its retail liquor license application 
was being considered for approval last year.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved 
to approve the renewal applications, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Turner 
provided additional information verifying only those with a Sam’s Club membership card could 
purchase alcohol products at the store, noting that several states where other Sam’s Club stores 
operate liquor package stores require that sales not be restricted to members only.  Upon further 
inquiry, he confirmed that the Cheyenne store is the only one that restricts sales to Club members 
only pursuant to verbal agree ment with the governing body when the license application was 
reviewed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. 
[CA] Retail liquor license transfer of ownership application filed with  the City Clerk’s Office 
for Lincolnway, Inc. d/b/a Lincolnway Liquor, 2825 East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(License currently held by Mason Properties, LLC dba Lincolnway Liquor, 282 5 E. Lincolnway, 
Cheyenne, WY).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Announcement was made of the Appointment of Frank Gerstenkorn to the Personnel 
Commission, to be confirmed March 27, 2017. 
 
 Under other business, Mr. Johnson provided an update on several community projects 
and fund-raising activities in which he is involved. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:19 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  March 21, 2017

ORDINANCE – 3  READING – Crea ting the Civic Center  Commons P rojectrd
Management Team and providing procedures for the operation thereof.  Jac k Speight spoke in
opposition of the ordinance .  Topics included in his comments were: Future developments and
improvements in the West Edge area; private investors; funding  sources; FEMA g rant and 6 th
penny funds; site remediation, environmental studies, and developing the area in phases.  During
his comments, Mr. Speight display ed and referenced an aerial map, and submitted informational
handouts (on file with C ity Clerk’s off ice).  Gene  MacD onald, Ay res Assoc iates, provided
detailed information on the project.  Topics included in his comments were : Expertise of Ayres
staff involved in the pr oject; environmental site assessments  and studies; voluntary remediation
program; FEMA grant and EPA revo lving loan program; cost bene fit ratios, and dra inage paths
and flood c ontrol measure s for the Capitol B asin, 19  and 26  St. drainag e proje cts and theth th
downtown a rea.  T he fol lowing e xpressed c oncerns w ith the  Civ ic Ce nter C ommons Pr oject,
including problems that had been raised relative to the use of federal funds and effect of reduced
parking for Ci vic Center pa trons:  Ba rbie Ha rrington, Major Dorr  and B rad H arrington.
Speaking in f avor of the project involving  redevelopment of the area and addressing fl ood
control problems were:  Dean Dexter and Dave Teubner, Warehouse 21; Carrie Hufney; Howard
Rodack; Tony  L ucero, A &  C Fee d, and J oe Doug herty.  Reporting  for Public Service s
Committee, Mr. Roy bal moved to appr ove o n third and f inal rea ding, se conded by Mr. Case.
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Discussion included c ontent
of the proposed substitute, which would create a Civic Center Commons Project Oversight Team
(CPOT), ins tead o f a P roject Ma nagement T eam, t o o versee the  a dministration o f t he pr oject;
provide disclosure of documentation to FEMA and an amended scope of services to be provided
by DHM De sign Corpor ation.  Commenting in support of the a mendment, including
redevelopment of the a rea and implementing  flood control mea sures were: Mr. Speig ht, Jordan
Dean, Danielle Ma rks, Tom Dickson and G len Garrett.  Discussion followed and included the
following topics: Lack o f t imeliness i n receiving the p roposed s ubstitute p rior t o t he m eeting;
transparency; FEMA involvement, a nd the importance of f lood control in the downtown ar ea.
Upon inquiry to City Council members and there being no objection voiced, Mayor Orr
expressed her concern with the lack of timeliness in receiving the proposed substitute for review;
provided infor mation on projec t funding  source s and status of the F EMA g rant, a nd posed
questions with regard to whether the substitute contained a sunset clause, whether DHM had
been contacted concerning a change in scope  of work unde r their c ontract, and whether it was
applicable to name a specific design company by name in an ordinance.  In response, Mr. Shanor
stated a sunse t provision was  contained in S ection 4(A) of the substitute; that DHM has be en
contacted and they are willing to look at additional services, and that DHM has a current contract
with the City specific to the project.  Upon  inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised that if
there is further a mendment made to Sec tion 4(A) cla rifying a n ordinanc e “sunse t” date, the re
shouldn’t be a major concern with referencing DHM in the ordinance.  Motion to amend carried.
Voting “y es” - - all member s of the g overning body  with the exception of Mr. Johns on and
Mayor Orr voting “ no”.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend by  adding the foll owing sentence at the
end of Section 4(A), seconded by Mr. Cook:  “This ordinance shall sunset upon completion of all
project activity and shall be of  no further  force and e ffect”.  Motion to amend c arried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 4(C) by adding
“and any other de partment that the pr oject oversight team deems nec essary” to t he end of  the
first sentence, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  D uring brief explanation of the intent of the proposed
amendment, Ms. Hackl offered to provide different wording on the topic.  Motion to amend
failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend Section
4(C) by revising the first sentenc e to read as follows, seconded by Mr. Roybal: “The CPOT and
DHM will work dire ctly with  any City depar tments and employ ees de emed ne cessary for the
completion of the project.”  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Main motion as amended by substitute, as twice amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Mayor Orr voting “no”. (#4170)
Following a brief minute recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:35 p.m.

Consideration of Bid #S-7-17 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Department, and Simon Contractors, Chey enne, for the Ca pitol Basin 26  Street I nterceptorth
Storm Sewer Project.  Ke lly Hafner, BenchMark Engineers, outlined benefits and me rits of the
project, noting the pr oject is fully  funded, a nd revie wed other  aff iliated floo dway mitigation
projects.  He a dvised the basis of the pr oject’s design is to optimiz e the volume of  flood wate r
runoff, and that expan sion is planned, contingent upon funding, to continue the proje ct eastward
into the Capitol Basin are a.  Mr. Ha fner discussed the ne ed to update the  Capitol Basin Master
Plan and indicate d the project will assist with flood control measure s.  Nanc y Loomis, building
owner in the project area, discussed flooding conditions affecting her business property; the need
to update the maste r plan; wa ter features and run- off contamination, and c ommented in support
of the project.  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $3,412,541.87, seconded
by Mr. Johnson.  The following topics were included in discussion on the item: Need to begin
planning to proce ed with next project phase to a ddress flooding  issues near the Capitol and
Herschler buildings; wetlands trea tment of stormwater runoff; revising the cur rent master plan;
funding concerns for futur e construction; alleviating f looding along the Snyder, O’Neil, Capitol
floodplain areas and downtown; changes in scope of work relative to E DA grant funding; cost
benefit analysis; extension of g rant time fr ame, and postponement of the item to allow time for
BenchMark to further  evalua te eleme nts involved in the project.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Haf ner
provided his profe ssional backg round qualifica tions and experience  and re sponded to querie s
pertaining to proje ct desig n; funding  time frame ; flood impacted are as; corre lation of the 26 th
5
Street Project with the Civic Center Commons Project and recently completed Sn yder/19th
Street Project, and c ost benefit ana lysis.  Upon inquiry , Ms. L oomis, commented on wate r
quality features near 23  Street and the need to include wetlands when updating the master plan.rd
Sam Berta, City Engineer’s office, provided information on the cha nge in scope of work f or the
project relative to the ED A grant;  EDA’ s awareness of the cha nge in the scope  of work; grant
time frame , and time frame involving the  bid submit ted by  the contra ctor.  Scott L arson,
BenchMark Engineers, explained initially the projec t was to extend to Carey Avenue, however,
funding was not sufficient to extend the projec t that distance so the improve ments will stop at
O’Neil Avenue f or this phase of  the proje ct.  Sy lvia Hac kl, Cit y Attorney , revie wed wr itten
communications by the City to EDA involving the change in scope of work.  Mr. Shanor moved
to postpone the item to May  8, 2017, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" --
all mem bers of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johns on and May or Orr voting
“no”.

downtown. Comments were  made on displac ing r esidents; proper ty locations and finding
funding to provide for  abatement.  May or Orr thanked Committee memb ers for their time a nd
work on the project.

member, who would like to pursue the establishment of a recreational trail system on the City-
owned ranch.  T odd Thibodeau discusse d trail system developments at Curt Go wdy State Park,

stop, and spoke in s upport of developing a trail system on the ranch for users seeking multiple

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming”, and refer the item to Public Services Committee, seconded by Mr.
Johnson.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, clarified that, procedurally, a motion isn’t needed f or Mr.
Shanor, as sp onsor, to introduce a n ordinanc e on 1  Reading , which will automatically  best
referred to one of  the Council’s  standing committees.  Mr. Shanor distributed copie s of the
proposed or dinance and advise d the purpose of  the ordinanc e is t o codify which depa rtment
directors would be required to be appointed by the Mayor, and confirmed by City Council, to
address t he Ma yor’s a dministrative re organization r elative to u pcoming bu dget di scussions.
Sylvia Hackl, City  Attorney, advised she had prepared two ordinances on the t opic.  One
ordinance conta ined only  those positions st atutorily mandated  to be appointed, w hich is the
version the Mayor supports, and the other ordinance, which Mr. Shanor introduced, includes the
statutorily required positions pl us all of the May or’s proposed department director positions per
the reorganization.
Mr. L aybourn commente d on a we stern water  sy stem confe rence he r ecently attended
and r ecognized t he B oard o f P ublic U tilities f or t heir p lanning a nd a wareness o f c ommunity
water issues.
There being  no further  business to come be fore the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:28 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: May 15, 2017 Special Meeting of the Governing Body | DATE: 2017-05-15 | CLIP_ID: 600 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 600

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 15, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date
beginning at Noon in Council Chambers for the purpose of considering a contract between the City
of Cheyenne and United Healthcare for group health insurance coverage for City employees.  Present
were:  MA YOR – M arian J. O rr; CO UNCIL ME MBERS – Rocky Case, B ryan Co ok, Pe te
Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal and Dicky Shanor.  Absent: Richard Johnson, Dr. Mark Rinne
and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.
The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Denise Freeman, Human Resources Director, reviewed employee healthcare plan coverage
since January 2016 when the City’s provider changed from WinHealth to Cigna, noting the coverage
through Cigna would expire on July 1 .  She outlined the process the City utilized to acquire quotesst
from providers due to Cigna’s notification that there would be a 63 percent increase (approximately
$5,000,000.00) in c ost to the City to c ontinue the current plan, which was not affordable.  M s.
Freeman explained the City’s utilization of Cobecon consultants, an employee benefit management
firm, to assist in discussion s with proposed provider s, including Cigna; review of proposals and
advice on a healthcare plan for employees.  Ms. Freeman advised the reason for conducting a special
City Council meeting  on the topic was to a cquire approval of a pr oposed contract with United
Healthcare, new plan provider, to a llow time for employee open enrollment so coverage could
continue under  the new pr ovider without a bre ak in cove rage.  C obecon repre sentatives Rob
Henderson and Diana Madvig provided a powerpoint presentation and discussed the City’s current
plan and costs; the proposal from United Healthcare, which closely equates to the current “Shelter
Plan” offered by Cigna, and responded to inquiries from Council members.  It was noted there would
be no cost increase to employees’ portion of health insurance costs under the United Healthcare plan.
Expressing concerns with the p roposed new healthca re plan were:  Stephanie L owe, Cheyenne
Public Employees Association; Jon Bertsch, Fire Fighters Union Local #279; Michael Gross, and
Mike Fernandez, Police officer.  Comments included concerns with the change in coverage options
and benefits; increased out-of-pocket employee costs;  lateness of notification of a new provider and
plan ch anges; lack of  involvement in the selec tion review pr ocess; vision cover age; no salar y
increases proposed to assist with higher deductible, co-pay and minimum amounts, and a past
insurance committee which included employee members and their opportunity to r eview health
coverage proposals.
Mr. Luna moved to approve the contract, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion included:
Cigna’s rate increase; coverage for same sex partners; types of healthcare plans available; wellness
program; City’s claims experience; process resulting in the selection of Cobecon as a consultant;
coverage for firefighter union members; Cobecon’ s marketing practices to ac quire clients, and
purpose of the special meeting.  Throughout the meeting information in response to comments and
inquiries was provided by Mayor Orr; Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney; Alessandra McCoy, Assistant
City Attorney; Ms. Freeman; Mr.  Henderson and Ms. Madvig.  During comments regarding action
on the contract at the special meeting, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, clarified that if the motion is
approved (or not approved) at the current meeting, the agenda item, i.e. contract,  would not be listed
on the Council’s May 22, 2017 meeting agenda as the special meeting is an official meeting of the
governing body .  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to May  22, 2017, seconde d by  Mr. Case.
Discussion followed on postponement allowing for information to be received on potential additional
plan options and related cost.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr voting “no”.
Purpose of the special meeting having been met, and there being no other business previously
noted for the special meeting, Mayor Orr adjourned the meeting at 1:34 p.m.
Submitted by,
Kylie Soden
Deputy City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
May 27, 2017

Contract between the City of Cheyenne and United Healthcare for group health insurance
coverage fo r C ity e mployees.  De nise F reeman, Human Re sources D irector, r eported th at,
pursuant to discussion and re quest for a dditional options for e mployee plan coverage, options
were prepared and distributed.  Diana  Madvig, Cobecon consultant, provided information on the
five different plan options (Orange,  Green, Purple, Brown and B lue plans); we llness plan; plan
rates; Affordable Care Act notifica tion requirements; i mportance of ha ving a sustainable plan;
deductible a nd co-pa ys; health reimburse ment acc ount (HRA) option;  recommendation of the
Blue plan; out-of-ne twork employ ee c osts, and impact of the City ’s hig h claims utiliz ation.
Stephanie Lowe, Interim President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA), expressed
appreciation for the  difficulty of the g overning body’s decision on an employ ee health plan a nd

A 201 Exception req uest to allow a septic w astewater tre atment sy stem for a  6,000-
square foot offic e building on a  7-ac re site loca ted within L aramie County jurisdiction but
contiguous to t he City of Cheyenne (located south of Dell Range Blvd. at it s intersection with
US-30 at the terminus of Hensley Road).  Andrea Shepard, property owner, expressed her belief
that they qualify for the 201 e xception due to the a mount it wil l cost to tap into the sewer  line
across the highw ay.  She stated the c ost could be approximately $300,000.00, not including the
cost of surve ys and ea sements, to install an 8" se wer line and a “g rinder”.  Susana Montana,
Development office, stated there  are two sewe r lines in prox imity to the parc el, one a t Whitney
Rd. and US-30 (2,200 linear ft. distant from the parcel) and a second one at Saddle Ridge Trail
and US-30 (320 linea r ft. in distance ).  She provided inf ormation on the shorter  option with an
open cut scenario, and advised City Engineer staff suggests the applicant did not investi gate the
shorter option, which should be a  lesser cost. Ms. Montana noted communication with WYDOT
was made as to whe ther they would permit extension of the sewer  line within US-30, by  either
boring under or  doing a n open cut in the hig hway, noting e ither would be approved but that an
open cut would be less expensive.  She re ported the shorter route option was not investigated by
the a pplicant’s e ngineer a nd sta ff, th erefore, f eels t he 20 1 e xception fo r eco nomic h ardship
purposes has not been met as financial hardship information for that option was not submitted.
She indicated the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) advised they could provide service to the site
and that B OPU had recommended the pr operty be anne xed into the City .  Ms. Shepard stated
they did check, after the fact, into the cost of the shorter route and to annex the property would
cost almost $1,000,000.00, noting that doing an open cut is expensive due to traffic management
costs while the work is conducted.  Dr. Rinne reported there was no recommendation on the item
from Public Service s Commi ttee.  Dr . Rinne moved to app rove, seconded by  Mr. J ohnson.
Discussion in cluded estimated costs re lated to the har dship ex ception re quest and a pplicant’s
availability to r eapply fo r t he re quest, if  the  mot ion fa iled, wi th a dditional fina ncial ha rdship
information.  Motion failed.  Voting  “yes” - - Mr. L aybourn.  Voting  “n o” --  Mr. Case, Mr.
Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.
Announcement was made of a Public He aring to be held Monday, June 12, 2017 at 6:00
p.m. in Ci ty Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue , for pr oposed budg ets for t he City  of
Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2018.
Announcement w as ma de of  the  Ap pointment o f Ja son B ogstie to t he Hi storic
Preservation Board, to be confirmed June 12, 2017.
Announcement was made that a Calcutta, benefitting the Multiple Sclerosis Society, will
be held Thur sday, J une 22, 2017 begin ning at 7:00 p.m. at Uncle Char lie’s L ounge, 6001
Yellowstone Roa d, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunc tion with the 23  annual Unc le Charlie’ srd
“DONE” golf tournament (15% of proceeds go to the Multiple Sclerosis Society). 
Under other business:
Mr. J ohnson inquired whethe r there  was intere st in reconsider ing the  vote on the
sanitation rate or dinance that failed a t the May  8, 2017  City Council meeting .  May or Orr
explained that a motion to reconsider would need to be made by a Council member who voted
on the prevailing side (i.e. one who voted “no”) on the ordinance.  No motion to reconsider was
offered.
There being  no further  business to come before the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 10:58 p.m.
Submitted by,
Kylie Soden

ORDINANCE –  3  RE ADING –  A ppropriating mo neys f or th e Ci ty o f Ch eyenne,rd

Consideration of B id #S-12-17 for a contra ct between the City  of Chey enne, Facilities
Maintenance Division, and Amer i-Tech HVAC Services, Inc., of Cheyenne, for mechanical and
HVAC systems maintenance for 14 City Buildings Project.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public
Works, advised bids were received from Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, Inc. and Long Building
Technologies, and explained bid require ments and irre gularities incurr ed by  both b idders
affiliated with the bidding pr ocess involving a bid being submitted to an incor rect location and
residency information.  She not ed that City attorney staff de termined that both compa nies are
considered to be resident businesses and that Ameri-Tech HVAC Services’ bid was the lowest
bid.  Bria n Dubois, L ong Building  Tec hnologies, provided an ove rview of  the company  and
services it could provide; expressed his belief that it would be unfair to award the bid to Ameri-
Tech, and provide d information on his company ’s bid and proc ess followed f or submitting the
bid.  During his comments, Mr. Dubois submitted a copy of a page from the City’s bid document
affiliated with proof of residency if a bidder was a Wyoming resident bidder (on file with City
Clerk’s office).  Jim Brown, Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, Inc., discussed elements of the City’s
bid document, the bidding process, and information submitted with his company’s proposal. Mr.
White moved to ac cept the bid f rom Ameri- Tech HVAC Service s, I nc. in  the amount of
$261,000.00, seconde d by  Mr. J ohnson.  Upon inquiry , Mr. B rown a dvised they  have been
awarded c ontracts t o p rovide the se se rvices f or ma ny y ears, n oting the City  g oes o ut t o b id
approximately every two to three y ears, and Sara  Vasq uez, Purchasing Manager, verified  that
bids f or a  c ontract a re re quested e very tw o y ears wi th n otices se nt t o n umerous bid ders in
addition to advertising, and that Ameri-Tech has been the sole bidder in recent years.  Discussion
included the City ’s bid proce ss and proc edures; reviewi ng practices utilized by  other
municipalities in the region; reviewing bids for best quality and performance and not necessarily
based upon the  lowest bid; fiscal saving s to the City  by  acc epting th e lowest bid, and re -
evaluating the RF P document for f uture bids.  Upon  inquiry during discussion, Ms. Nemece k
stated that the bid is fairly  standard f or HVA C service s; advised that the RFP (Reque st for
Proposals) is updated e very two years with information pertaining  to the fac ilities involved, and
indicated many upgrades to HVAC systems have been made over the years to i mprove facility
efficiencies.  Mr. Dubois provided information on why Long Building Technologies h asn’t
submitted a bid in the past, and differences they have noticed in RFP’s outside of the City which
are more performance based and not based on lowest price.  U pon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City
Attorney, explained that state statutes require approval of the lowest responsive bidder for public
works projects but it’s unclear what constitutes “public works”.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. L aybourn, Mr. Shanor a nd Mayor
Orr voting “no”. (#6675)

ORDINANCE –  3  REA DING - P ursuant t o Section 2 .2.1 Zoning Ma p A mendments,rd
