RESOLUTION – Aut horizing the May or and the  City  Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for
Arroyo Subdivision, a re plat of all of B lock 4 (Lots 1 thru 9 inclusive) and those a djacent alley
rights-of-way, Spring Valley Addition, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at
the southeast cor ner of  the intersec tion of Townsend Place  and Mar jon Co urt).  De b Roden,
Woodhouse Roden Nethercott law firm, attorney representing the applicant, provided an update
on a proposed map revision for 10 lots to be developed and addressing drainage issues, which
includes a spe cific note (i.e. Note  10) which r eflects the intent that th e lots will  be deve loped
under standards of the UDC (Unified Development Code) Section 5.1.5 for DD7-single family
detached residential dwellings.  She noted, if appr oved, the applicant will be see king a variance
through the Board of Adjustment.  Mitch Gerharter inquired as to the next steps procedurally if
the plat is approved , improvements to the drainage area that would be dedicated to the City, and
whether t here wa s a  City  governed timeframe fo r c onstruction o f t he de velopment p roject.
Mayor Orr advised it was not unusual for  open space to be donated to the City  for a project and
that she wa s not awar e of a  deadline f or projec t construction.  Seth L loyd, Pla nning and
Development, explained the next step, if the resolution is approved, is for the applicant to apply
for a variance in lot size with the Boar d of Adjustment.  I f that’s approved, then a n engineering
plan review for drainage improvements will be conducted, and then the building permit process
will follow.  He noted the dr ainage improvements are the  responsibility of the de veloper.  Mr.
Lloyd added that he is also unawa re of  any  City  g overned timelines for construc tion.  Mr.
Roybal moved to adopt, sec onded by Mr. J ohnson.  Mr. Roy bal, seconded by Mr. Case, moved
to amend by revised plat map dated June 22, 2017 and by adding the following language to Note
10: “The lots containe d within this  plat shall be conside red a s developa ble pursuant to the
standards of UDC 5.1.5 DD7-single family detached dwelling/small lot for ten (10) single family
residential units.”  I t was noted the amendment to Note 10 was adde d at the re quest of the
applicant to provide intent to the Board of Adjustment that the governing body was in support of
the development.  Mr. Lloyd stated the revised map provides clarification on some prior issues,
however, staff is not in favor of including  Note #10, either  as initiall y proposed or as a mended,
and staff  is recommending  denial of the  plat as a w hole as the pr oposed lots are  not of a
buildable size within t he cur rent zoning district.  Upon i nquiry, Alessandr a McCoy, Assistant
City Attorney , explained the City  isn’t nece ssarily opposed but the note wording  isn’t
enforceable and sug gested Note  10 lang uage could be  altere d to refle ct the governing  body ’s
intent i nstead of ha ving the  word “ shall” in the wording .  Motion to amend car ried.  Voting
“yes” -- all member s of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson
voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor, seconded by Dr. Rinne, moved to ame nd Note 10 by striking the word
“shall” and inserting the phrase “it is t he intent of the governing body that they” so the sentence
would instead re ad as f ollows: “The lots contained w ithin thi s plat, it is the intent of the
governing body that they be considered as developable pursuant to the standards of UDC 5.1.5
DD7-single fa mily detached swe lling/small lot for ten (10)  single  family  residential units.”
Following discussion, moti on to amend c arried.  Voting “ yes” -- all member s of the g overning
body with the exception of Mr. Johns on v oting “no”.  It was noted the a mendments wer e a
compromise for  the prope rty owner /developer and the ne ighborhood but it places staff  in a
difficult posit ion. Main motion as twice ame nded carried. Voting “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Mayor Orr voting “no”. (#5862)

Retail liquor license transfer of ownership application filed with the  City  Clerk’s Of fice
for Maverik, Inc.  d/b/a Maverik, Inc. #426, 140 Gardenia Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License
currently he ld b y L ambousis Revocable Tr ust, d /b/a Th e Ma yflower, 11 2 We st 1 7th Str eet,
Cheyenne, WY).  Bud dy Tennant spoke in opposition of the license tra nsfer to a conve nience
store environment and submitted a  written letter of protest in support of his comments (on file in
City Clerk’s off ice).  David Ha ncock, Vice President and g eneral counse l, Maverik, I nc.,
provided information on Maverik’ s business opera tions and submitt ed and dis cussed a handout
(on file with City  Clerk’s off ice) which co ntained additional information from the one  that was
submitted at the previous City Council meeting.  He commented in support of t he transfer which
will allow a small packa ge store w ithin the buildi ng, an d incremental sales tax reve nue for
Cheyenne.  Chuc k West, J ohn Lambousis and Steve B orin spoke in  favor of the tr ansfer, citing
recent state statute c hanges involving “pa rked” liquor licenses; history  of the re tail license;
additional sales tax revenue to be  g enerated by having  an ac tive license; a ddressing un derage
drinking concerns; no interest having been expressed by anyone interested in acquiring the
license for use in the downtown area, and previous transfer applications for the license that have
not been a pproved.  K evin Geor ges discussed  the re striction on the number of r etail liquor
licenses available through the City due to the state sta tute population formula; ba sing a decision
on whether to approve the application on what’s best for Cheyenne, and conditions set forth in
Wyo. Stat. Section 12-4-104 (b)(iii) regarding whether approval of the license meets the needs of
the vicinity .  Mr. White moved t o approve, seconded by  Mr. L una.  Upon inquiry , Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, advised th e lic ense is c urrently in “ parked” sta tus a nd un der t he ne wly
revised statute provisions, the license c ould come bac k to the City  during  the 2018 licensing
renewal process as the timeframe to extend the “pa rked” status has changed, and commented on
the de finition of  “ operational”.  D iscussion in cluded the fo llowing: A ntiquated s tate sta tute
structure regarding liquor licenses; financial value to holders of a retail liquor license; having a
willing seller and willing buyer and free enterprise; whether transfer of the license will increase
sales tax revenue ; whether the ar ea is a lready served by exis ting licensees; status of “ parked”
licenses; no opposition to the transfer received from residents or liquor licensees in the area; data
submitted by Maverik’s supporting the transfer and Maverik’s preliminary review of potentially
requesting transfer of the license for use at it’s east Cheyenne business location.  U pon inquiry,
Ms. Intlekofer advised ther e are approximately four r etail licenses currently in “parked” status
and not all license s are on the same status e nding timeframe.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” --
Mr. Cook, Mr. J ohnson, Mr. L aybourn, Mr. L una, Mr. Roy bal, Mr. Shanor a nd Mr. White.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Case, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Orr. 
Under other business:
Ellen Stomp i nquired a bout the scope of work to be inc luded in the traf fic study  for
Cheyenne Frontier Days, noting that pedestrian and bicyclist traffic need to be included in the
study and Eighth Avenue crosswalks should be considered.  Vi cki Nemecek, Director of Public
Works, provided information on a pr oposed painted c rosswalk (north-south) on the ea st side of
Carey and Eig hth Avenues.  Ma yor Orr  advised the study  is thro ugh the MP O (Metr opolitan
Planning Organization) and CFD  and includes pedestrian traffic and was approved pursuant to
action on the Consent Agenda earlier in the meeting.  Mr. Laybourn commented that members of
the community should be able to request removal of items from the Consent Agenda.
Don Pierson discussed a  safety issue with alley  traffic near the 4400 block of  East 17 th
Street (and Cheyenne Ranch area) off the Greenway path that is experiencing speeding, careless
driving and unsaf e vehicle traffic resulting in safe ty concerns for the  residential neighborhood.
He stated drivers are using the alley as a shor tcut route and due to the configuration of the a lley
numerous ac cidents have occurred in whic h the City ’s split rail fenc e has be en dama ged and
vehicles have wound up down the slope into the back yards of residences.  Mr. Pierson requested
the alley be made a one-way with barricading on the east end and signage at the west end that no
through traffic was allowed.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, advised that the alley is
approximately 1,300 ft. in length and slopes d ownhill and that she had visited with the traffic
engineer about potentially  blocking  the alley  either on the  east e nd or in th e middle and that
further r esearch w ould b e c onducted.  Cor ey Ki mball e xpressed s imilar s afety c oncerns;
reviewed accident history over the years, and residents’ concerns, and encouraged closure of the
alley to throug h traffic.  I n support of his comments, Mr. Kimball submitted a written proposa l
and pe tition c oncerning t he un paved a ccess r oad ( on fi le wi th C ity Cle rk’s of fice).  Kr istin
Russell, referencing her email to governing body members (on file with City Clerk’s office), also
spoke about the dangerous conditions involving vehicle traffic and expressed support to close the
alley to traffic.  [City Clerk note: Due to a previous commitment, Mr. Shanor left the meeting] 
 
Reminder was made of the annual Police Department sponsored Neighborhood Nig ht-
Out community block parties to be held July 11 .  M r. Johnson noted an upcoming communityth
event involving collection of non-perishable goods and requested an update on the status of the
electrical a nd la ndscaping wo rk a t th e ro undabout n ear S outh H igh Sc hool.  I n r esponse to
comment regarding City-owned bricks stored in the dow ntown area, Vicki Nemecek, Director of
Public Works, advised City staff is addressing the problem.  Appreciation was expressed to staff
for researching the alley issue raised by concerned residents.
There being n o f urther b usiness to  c ome b efore th e g overning b ody, th e me eting was
officially adjourned at 9:40 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk

misperception related to City Council’s involvement in the project; neighborhood homeowners’

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-08-28 | CLIP_ID: 615 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 615

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 28, 2017
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS
– Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dr. Mark
Rinne (arriving at 6:03 p.m.), Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City
Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [C A] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. Cook and Mr. White, Agenda Item #22 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Shanor
recused himself from voting participation on the item due to an affiliation with the State Land and
Investment Board (Consent Agenda Item #18), and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved
to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement
of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers.  
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 14, 2017.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. J ohnson moved to suspend the rules to c hange the o rder of the a genda by moving
Agenda Item #22 (Resolution proclaiming “Purple Heart Day” and the City as a Purple Heart City)
to be acted upon prior to Agenda Item #7 (Vouchers), seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
RESOLUTION – Proclaiming ‘Purple Heart Day’ and the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to
be a Purple Heart City.  Speaking in support of the resolution were:  Brian Olson, Commander, local
chapter of the Military Order of the Purple Heart; Retired Lt. Col. Barry Gostas; Larry Barttelbort,
Director, Wyoming Veterans Commission; Ted Gostas; Floyd Watson, Save American Veterans;
Major General Luke Reiner, Wyoming Military Department; Lisa Olson Snyder and Peter Howard.
Topics discussed included: History of the Purple Heart medal; recipient qualifications; recognition
of the City as the first city in Wyoming to be designated as a Purple Heart City; importance of the
City’s military support in Cheyenne, and consideration of veterans for positions of leadership and
service on boards and committees.  Ted Gostas commented on his military experience, including
his time as a POW (prisoner of war) which resulted in his receiving two Purple Heart medals.  Mr.
Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Discussion included expression of appreciation to
those serving in the milit ary and particularly to Mr. (Ted) Gostas for his leadership and courage.
It was noted that community events will be planned to celebrate Purple Heart Day in 2018.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5872)
 Mr. Luna moved to appr ove payment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Cook.
Inquiry wa s ma de re garding vo uchers pa yable to K utak Ro ck, L LP ( professional se rvices in
connection with calcu lation of arbitrage rebate on pa rking structure bonds) and the pur pose of
services provided, and AVI Engineering (professional services for the 17  St. Streetscape Project),th
regarding project oversight and sustainable life of the infrastructure.  Sylvia  Hackl, City Attorney,
advised further information on servic es by Kutak Rock would be  provided to g overning body
members, and Jim Voeller, City Engineer, provided information available on the 17  St. Streetscapeth
Project.  Mr. L una moved to re move the voucher payable to Kutak Rock L LP from the vouche r
payment list, seconded by  Mr. J ohnson.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body.  Main motion to approve, with the exception of the voucher payable to Kutak Rock
LLP, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson
voting “no”.

RESOLUTION – Authorizing a  street name change for a portion of Summit Drive ly ing
between Ridge Road and Colleg e Drive, City of Chey enne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Tom
Mason, Director, Metropolitan Planning Office, referenced and discussed a map he had previously
provided to governing body members (on file with City Clerk’s office), and explained the intent of
the resolution is to provide consistency in the street name for Storey Blvd. corridor, which will also
assist with emergency response services for the area. He advised there wer 18 addresses that would
be affected by the name change and time would be provided for residents to change their address
information re lative to mail del ivery.  He noted there  is another Summit Drive, which isn’t
connected to this portion of Summit Drive, elsewhere in the City that further convolutes the issue.
Mr. Roybal reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Comm ittee.
Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  U pon inquiry, Mr. Mason stated the GIS
system can be quickly updated as soon as it is legally appropriate to do so, and that contact will be
made to Goog le maps about upda ting its mapping  information.  Comment was made r egarding
planned apartment units slated to be built in the area and making the street name change prior to
additional development along the corridor.  Motion carr ied.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#5875)
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to execute a Wyoming State
Historical Records Advisory Board grant agreement for digitization of historic Cheyenne building
plans.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5876)

a joint application for a grant award under the Edward Byrne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant

2018.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6715)
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Cheyenne
Firefighters Local Union No. 279, I nternational Association of Fi refighters, for the purpose of
clarifying the intent of Section 23, Working in a Higher Job Rank.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve,
seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to September 11, 2017 pursuant to staff
request, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body. 
[CA] Agreement for Juvenile Probation Services between Laramie County, Wyoming and the City
of Cheyenne, office of Youth Alternatives.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6716) 
[CA] Co-sponsorship of a Gr ant Ag reement (along  with L aramie Coun ty and the Chey enne
Regional Airport Board) with the Federal Aviation Administration for Federal Aviation Grant AIP
#53 for the new Airport Terminal Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6717) 
[CA] Agreement to donate real property between the City of Cheyenne and Lowe’s Home Centers,
LLC, pertaining to the Christensen Road Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6718)
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County School District Number One ,
Sunflower Ridge L3C, and the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, for drainage improvements, Buffalo
Ridge Subdivision – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 6457 between the City of Cheyenne and FCI
Constructors of Wyoming LLC, for additions and renovations to the Waste Transfer Station Project
– referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and DHM
Design Corporation to move funds fr om Task 4, Constr uction Administration, to Tasks 1 and 2,
Preliminary and Final Design,  for engineering design services for the Civic Center Commons / West
Edge Project – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of B id #S-1-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Department, a nd Simo n Co ntractors, Che yenne, WY , f or the  20 17 Chi p Sea l, P art #2  Pr oject.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $814,363.00). (#6719)
[CA] Annual report to the City of Cheyenne regarding capital improvements made at the Airport
Golf Cou rse by the  Ai rport G olf Clu b a s r equired b y Lease Agreem ent N o. 28 33.  M otion to
acknowledge receipt of the annual report (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, noted t he following was inadvertently not designated as a
Consent Agenda item and read the title of the agenda item:  Community Facilities Fees Report for
Fiscal Year 2016-2017.  Mayor Orr referred the item to Finance Committee.
Announcement was made that due to the Labor Day holiday on Monday, September 4, 2017,
regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instea d be held in t he M unicipal
Building, Room 122, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday , September 5, 2017 a t 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will meet
Wednesday, September 6, 2017 at Noon.
Under other business, Jeff Dierksen discussed traffic issues on P into Lane; referenced a
petition he filed last fall regarding mitigation of traffic conditions in the area, and requested an
update on  wh ether WY DOT h ad b een contacted r egarding int erpretation b y pr evious City
engineering sta ff of  the City ’s authority  to change  the speed limi t to 25 m.p.h. in residential
neighborhoods.  Mr. Dierksen voiced concerns that conditions have deteriorated for years due to
development in the area increasing the traffic volume and speeding by drivers accessing the area.
Upon inquiry, Jim Voeller, advised he was unaware if WYDOT had been contacted; stated the City
has done tra ffic studies in th e neighborhood with law e nforcement involvement, and note d the
position of traffic engineer is no longer under the Engineering Department.  Discussion included:
Potential options to reduce speeding on Pinto Lane; street classification, history of development in
the area affecting traffic conditions, and state law and jurisdictional issues concerning changes to
speed limits in neighborhoods.
There being  no further  business to come before the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:03 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
September 9, 2017

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-09-11 | CLIP_ID: 617 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 617

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 11, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dr. Ma rk 
Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent: Richard Joh nson.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, 
City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
  
 Mayor Orr called for a moment of silence in remembrance of the anniversary of the 
September 11, 2001 national tragedy. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr . Shanor, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item # 16 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Shanor recused himself from voting participation on the Consent Agenda items due to an affiliation 
with the State Land and Investment Board (Consent Agenda Item #22), and vacated Council 
Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on August 28 , 2017.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (voucher payable to himself) and vacated Council 
Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek,  Director of Public Works, provided information on past 
and current funding source for service s by Goat Green, LLC (vegetation management along Dry 
Creek and Crow Creek), and purpose of a professional services agreement with Tabitha Merkow 
(Fleet Maintenance personnel training).  Comment was made regarding future review of fund ing 
that may be available for goat grazing services.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of 
the governing body present  in Council Chambers .  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. 
Roybal returned to Council Chambers.   
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 5.6.2. 
PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Unified Development Code, changing the 
zoning classification from PUD Planned Unit Development to PUD Planned Unit Development 
for a portion of Lot 2, Block 1 of Buffalo Ridge Estates, 10th Filing, City of Ch eyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Sunflower Road, south of Sagebrush Ave).   
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Support was voiced for the development project planned for the property.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4198)  
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING –Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 5.1 of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code (UDC) to allow utility stations within the AG, AR, and AD zoning districts after an 
administrative use approval.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4199) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING –Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 1.4.4 and 5.1 of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC) to allow home occupations as a permitted use within the NB, CB, CBD, 
LI, and HI zoning districts.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4200) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the S½SE¼ Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to Dell Range Boulevard, east of Griffith Avenue).  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4201) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County MR Medium -density 
Residential to MR Medium-density Residential for a portion of the S½SE¼ Section 22, Township 
14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and 
adjacent to Dell Range Boulevard, east of Griffith Avenue).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on  
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna .  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of 
the governing body present. (#4202) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending S ections 1.4.3 and 5.7.1 of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC) to clarify the definition of ‘through lot’ for accessory structure setback 
purposes.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on seco nd reading, seconded by Mr. Case .  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from A -1 – Agriculture and Rural 
Residential (County) to AR Agricultural Residential for all of Blocks 146, 147, 148 and 149, 
Inclusive, Interior Heights, Second Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming; along 
with all of those adjacent rights-of-way contained within the plat boundary (including all of those 
intervening 15' alleys and all of those intervening portions of Kelley Avenue, Gadds Avenue and 
Holmes Avenue); and the remaining one -half (1/2) of those adjacent rights -of-way as shown 
(including E.40' York Avenue, N.30' 12th Street, W.40' Talbot Avenue and S.30' 11th Street), City 
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located to the northwest of the intersection of W. 
College Drive and Grand Harmony Boulevard) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Repealing Chapter 5.32, Bingo and Pull Tabs, in its 
entirety; amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally,  Section 5.04.030, Types of 
businesses, activities and events, and Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.050, 
Bingo an d pull tabs, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 9.08, 
Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, Section 9.08.030, Gambling-Permitted if authorized 
by state law-Limitation, and repealing Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, 
Section 9.08.040, Violations by owner or occupant of premises where gambling conducted, of 
Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming  – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the addition of twenty -nine (29) structures and removal of 
two (2) structures in the Downtown Cheyenne Historic District from the Contributing Structures 
List maintained by the City’s Planning office.  Stephanie Lowe, Planning and Development office, 
provided an update on the status of communication with property owners whose structures are 
involved in either being added to or removed from the List.  Christina Bird, Chairperson, Cheyenne 
Historic Preservation Board, explained compliance with federal requirements relating to the “opt 
in” provision had been met, noting that property owners who have not “opted out” will have their 
properties automatically included on the List.  Amy Smith commented on a Downtown Cheyenne 
Historic District report with regard to classification of properties  and the contributing rating 
system.  She submitted a copy of her comments (on file with City Clerk’s office) and requested 
consideration by the governing  body to reclassify the building referred to as Bart’s F lea Market, 
owned by her parents, from C -2, Contributing, to NC -1, Noncontributing Nonintrusive.  She 
explained that “opting out”, which they have done,  from being included in the List  is different 
from how the building is classified .  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lowe provided information on the “opt 
in/opt out” process; noted the classification report has no real authority, and stated if the Smith’s 
decided in the future they would like to have the building added to the List they can request to do 
so.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.   Ms. 
Bird, upon inquiry provided further explanation of federal (Secretary of the Interior) standards 
regarding the “opt-out” provision.  Comment followed on the number of communication letters 
that have not yet been returned to Ms. Lowe by property owners, and additional communication 
efforts by staff.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone to September 25, 2017, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Upon inquiry, Ms. Lowe advised she was still waiting to hear from nine property owners and her 
most recent letters requested a response by August 31, 2017, and that the purpose of the resolution 
was not time sensitive.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing 
body present. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Interior 
Heights, Fourth Filing, a replat of all of Blocks 146, 147, 148 and 149, Inclusive, Interior Heights, 
Second Filing, City of Cheyenne,  Laramie County, Wyoming; along with all of those adjacent 
rights-of-way contained within the plat boundary (including all of those intervening 15' alleys and 
all of those intervening portions of Kelley Avenue, Gadds Avenue and Holmes Avenue); and the 
remaining one-half (1/2) of those adjacent rights-of-way as shown (including E.40' York Avenue, 
N.30' 12th Street, W.40' Talbot Avenue and S.30' 11th Street), City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located to the northwest of the intersection of W. College Dri ve and Grand Harmony 
Boulevard) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Pershing Marketplace, 1st Filing, a replat o f all of Block 1 (Lots  1 thru 4, inclusive), and all of 
Block 2 (Lots 1 thru 6, inclusive), and all of those 20’ alley rights-of-way, adjacent to said Blocks 
1 and 2, and all of May Place (60’ R/W), adjacent to said Blocks 1 and 2, McCann Homesites, 
City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection 
of E. Pershing Boulevard and N. College Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Mission 
Village, situated in a portion of the S½SE¼ of Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of 
the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to Dell 
Range Boulevard, east of Griffith Avenue) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor  –  
Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Thanking the Cheyenne 150 th Anniversary Celebration Steering 
Committee – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Joining the City of Cheyenne to a Community -Wide Visioning and 
Planning Initiative – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Dr. Rinne, Mr. 
Roybal, Mr. Shanor) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming State Loan 
and Investment Board for a $1.6 Million Loan from the Clean W ater State Revolving Fund for 
funding the Dry Creek Interceptor Rehabilitation and auth orizing the Board Director to sign all 
loan documents – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Cheyenne 
Firefighters Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, for the purpose of 
clarifying the intent of Section 23, Working in a Higher Job Rank.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6725) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County School District Number One, 
Sunflower Ridge L3C, and the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, for drainage improvements, Buffalo 
Ridge Subdivision.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6726) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 6457 between the City of Cheyenne and FCI 
Constructors of Wyoming LLC, for additions and renovations to the  Waste Transfer Station 
Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6457)  
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and DHM 
Design Corporation to move funds from Task 4, Construction Administration, to Tasks 1 and 2,  
Preliminary and Final Design, for engineering design services for the Ci vic Center Commons / 
West Edge Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6498) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract 6628 between the City of Cheyenne and Pole 
Mountain Electric, Inc. for the Dell Range/Yellowstone Signal Upgrade Project. (2 011-2014 1% 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment to Contract No. 6630, Professional Services Agreement for Benefit 
Consulting Services between the City of Cheyenne and NOVO Benefits LLC (f/k/a Corp orate 
Benefits Consortium, LLC) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Memorandum of Understanding (Contract No. 6680) between the 
City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Laramie County, Wyoming, to provide public 
transportation services to Laramie County Community College and for an area of Laramie County 
immediately adjacent to the City. (Revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
Approval of Appointment of City Officials:  Robert Briggs, Director of the Planning and 
Development Department ; Ronald Downey , City Tr easurer; Greg Hoggatt Sr. , Chief of the 
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, and Teresa Moore, Director of the Community Recreation 
and Events Department.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who 
did not vote (Mayoral appointees).  Mayor Orr announced the oath of office for the new department 
directors would be administered tomorrow morning, September 12th, at 9:00 a.m. at the Cheyenne 
Public Safety Center and Council members, staff and the public were welcome to attend .  Mayor 
Orr introduced the new directors. 
[CA] Community Facilities Fees Report for Fiscal Year 2016 -2017.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Laybourn commented on a recent  situation in volving the City Engineer and his 
personal attorney; noted that word has been received from the FEMA Region 8 office that the cost/ 
benefit analysis (for the Civic Center Commons Project)  has met the standard , and requested an 
executive session of the governing body be scheduled in the near future to discuss affiliated 
personnel and legal matters.   Also under other business, Mr. White stated for the record that the 
word from FEMA proves no fraud by the City on the matter. 
 
 T.J. Kallas expressed student pedestrian safety concerns involving traffic at St. Mary’s 
School, particularly on O’Neil Street, and requested flashing and children playing signage be 
installed, and crosswalks around the school be painted.  Mayor Orr advised she would discuss the 
topic with staff. 
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:45 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-09-25 | CLIP_ID: 621 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 621

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 25, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00 
p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky 
Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dr. Ma rk Rinne, Dicky 
Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. 
 
 Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Orr welcomed and recognized dignitaries from the City 
of Cheyenne’s Sister City Accra, Ghana, and expressed appreciation for their efforts in traveling to 
Cheyenne.  Mayor Orr shared information on commonalities between the two cities and introduced the 
Mayor of Accra, the Honorable Mohammed Adjei Sowah, who introduced each dignitary in attendance 
and their area of legislative or administrative oversight. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be routine 
items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate discussion on 
these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other members is 
received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal sequence on t he 
agenda.)  Upon request by Mr . Shanor, and pursuant to support received by Mr.  Case and Mr. Roybal , 
Agenda Item #12 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to 
support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. White, Agenda Item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn , and pursuant to support received by Mr.  Case and Dr. Rinne, Agenda 
Item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. White, Agenda Item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon 
request by Mr. Shanor , and pursuant to support received by Mr.  Cook and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item 
#22(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Shanor recused himself from voting participation on 
Consent Agenda items due to an affiliation with the State Land and Investment Board (Consent Agenda 
Item #18), and vacated Council Cha mbers.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining Conse nt Agenda 
items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present 
in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers. 
 
 Mr. Laybourn moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda by moving Agenda 
Item #16 (Resolution Thanking the Cheyenne 150 th Anniversary Celebration Steering Committee) to be 
forwarded as the next item to be acted upon, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all 
members of the governing body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Thanking the Cheyenne 150 th Anniversary Celebration S teering Committee.  
Appreciation was expressed to Committee Coordinator Mona Pearl and all members of the Committee for 
their service in organizing and contributing to the success of the numerous celebration events.  Following 
the reading of the Resolution  in its entirety  by Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal, r eporting for Public Services 
Committee, moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the 
governing body. (#5878) 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on September 11 , 2017.   Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy Dodge, Inc.) and 
vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by 
Mr. White. Inquiry was made regarding vouchers payable to:  Laramie County Community Partnership 
(consultant services affiliated with Cohort 11 funding for Youth Alternatives ); Officials for Fun Club 
(officiating services for 2017 volleyball season and training session); Stalker Radar (new radar detection  
equipment for Police vehicles), and  Wyoming Bank and Trust (payment on refunding and improvement 
revenue bonds/parking structures).  Providing information available on the vouchers of interest were:  Ron 
Downey, City Treasurer; Teresa Moore, Director, Community Recreation and Events, who noted they 
would provide additional information relative to the vouchers for Laramie County Community Partnership 
and Officials for Fun Club, and Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department.  Comment was made regarding 
the voucher payable to Yuckos, Inc. and the cost of dog refuse litter bags for public parks.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present  in Council Chambers .  Following 
announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Chambers. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code (UDC), amending Sections 1.4.3 and 5.7.1 of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code (UDC) to clarify the definition of ‘through lot’ for accessory structure setback purposes.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all 
members of the governing body. (#4203) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 
5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified Development 
Code, changing the zoning classification from A -1 – Agriculture and Rural Residential (County) to AR 
Agricultural Residential for all of Blocks 146, 147, 148 and 149, Inclusive, Interior Heights, Second 
Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming; along with all of those adjacent rights -of-way 
contained within the plat boundary (inclu ding all of those intervening 15' alleys and all of those 
intervening portions of Kelley Avenue, Gadds Avenue and Holmes Avenue); and the remaining one-half 
(1/2) of those adjacent rights -of-way as shown (including E.40' York Avenue, N.30' 12th Street, W.4 0' 
Talbot Avenue and S.30' 11th Street), City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located to the 
northwest of the intersection of W. College Drive and Grand Harmony Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved 
to approve on second reading  with staff conditions #1 throu gh 3, seconded by Mr. Cook .  Upon inquiry 
regarding potential conflict with similar street names, Eric Hammer, Planning and Development  office, 
clarified an affiliated plat would vacate existing streets and did not dedicate new streets so street names 
were not an issue.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Repealing Chapter 5.32, Bingo and Pull Tabs, in its entirety; 
amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally,  Section 5.04.030, Types of businesses, activities 
and events, and Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.050, Bingo and pull tabs, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, 
Section 9.08.030, Gambling-Permitted if authorized by state law-Limitation, and repealing Chapter 9.08, 
Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, Section 9.08.040, Violations by owner or occupant of 
premises where gambling conducted, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, reviewed provisions contained in state 
statute (Wyo. Stat. § 6-7-101 et seq.) compared to current city code addressing gambling and bingo/pull 
tabs games, and recent state law change re moving the prohibition on gambling in licensed liquor 
establishments, noting illegal gambling is addressed as a violation elsewhere in state law.  He noted state 
law adequately covers concerns that years ago were put into place in city code, advising the provisions in 
city code were out of date as state law ha s since been changed to be more specific.  He further advised 
there are currently eight business licenses issued by the City to eligible non-profit organizations engaging 
in bingo/pull tab activities, and applying provisions of state law only would be less confusing for those 
conducting these types of games and less of a burden on law enforcement relative to staffing resources.  
Chief Kozak stated the Police Department will continue to investigate gaming issues on an as needed basis 
and the proposed ordinance will still allow law enforcement officers to cite violators into either Municipal 
Court or Circuit Court , and provided information on games of skill that are starting to appear in many 
community business establishments.  An explanation of proposed changes to City Code Title 9 regarding 
allowable gambling when a bona fide social relationship exists was also provided by Chief Kozak.  Mr. 
Roybal reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Case .  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak explained , if 
the ordinance was approved,  individuals would need to conduct their own research and make their own 
determination on whether certain types of electronic machines were legal and not considered gambling 
machines pursuant to state law provisions, and if any machines were found to be illegal, law enforcement 
would still have the authority to investigate and seize the machines.  Also upon inquiry, Chief Kozak 
stated his belief that under state law an eligible non-profit organization wishing to conduct pull tab games 
must do so only on premises owned or occupied by the nonprofit organization.   Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor 
and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Comment was made that there may be an amendment brought forward at the next Finance 
Committee meeting.  Motion carried.  Voting “y es” -- all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the addition of twenty-nine (29) structures and removal of two (2) 
structures in the Downtown Cheyenne Historic District from the Contributing Structures List maintained 
by the City’s Planning office. Stephanie Lowe, Planning and Development office, provided an update on 
the status of properties included on the list ; properties whose owners have not responded to “opt out” 
letters, and communication efforts made by the City’s Historic Preservation Board .  She explained there 
are now 20 structures to be added and 12 structures to be removed.  Amy Smith, on behalf of the property 
owner of 415 W. 16th Street, Bart’s Flea Market building, designated as building #100D in the consultant’s 
report, requested City Council reclassify the building from a C-2 (Contributing) designation, as listed in 
the consultant’s report, to a NC-1 (Noncontributing Nonintrusive) status to avoid any unknown impact 
the classification may have on property taxes and insurance.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lowe explained the consultant’s recommended classification of buildings has 
no impact as the report is not recorded or filed, and structures on the Contributing List would only be 
affected if demolition or building moving permits were involved.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the table 
of properties by adding an asterisk in the far right corner next to Bart’s Flea Market line and placing a 
corresponding asterisk at the end of the table with the wording “* Property owner has requested a NC -1 
(Noncontributing Nonintrusive) class ification”, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to amend  carried.  
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr.  Johnson voting “ no”.  
Concern was expressed with inclusion of buildings on the Contributing List without approval by the 
property owner.  Upon request for clarification, Ms. Lowe verified are now 20 structures to be added to 
the list with 12 removed.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend  by changing the number of structures authorized 
to be added to the Contributing List for the Do wntown Cheyenne Historic District from 29 to 20 and 
changing the number of structures to be removed from 2 to 12, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion as twice amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Shanor voting 
“no”. (#5879) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Interior 
Heights, Fourth Filing, a replat of all of Blocks 146, 147, 148 and 149, Inclusive, Interior Heights, Second 
Filing, City of Cheyenne,  Laramie County, Wyoming; along with all of those adjacent rights -of-way 
contained within the plat boundary  (including all of those intervening 15' alleys and all of tho se 
intervening portions of Kelley Avenue, Gadds Avenue and Holmes Avenue); and the remaining one-half 
(1/2) of those adjacent rights-of-way as shown (includirong E.40' York Avenue, N.30' 12th Street, W.40' 
Talbot Avenue and S.30' 11th Street), City of Chey enne, Laramie County, Wyomin g (located to the 
northwest of the intersection of W. College Dri ve and Grand Harmony Boulevard).  Mr. Roybal moved 
to adopt with staff conditions # 1 through 3, seconded by Mr. Case .  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to 
October 9, 2017, seconded by Mr. Luna.  It was noted a postponement would allow for the affiliated zone 
change request (Agenda Item #14) to catch up procedure-wise with the Final Plat resolution.  Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Pershing 
Marketplace, 1st Filing, a replat of all of Block 1 (Lots 1 thru 4, inclusive), and all of Block 2 (Lots 1 thru 
6, inclusive), and all of those 20’ alley rights-of-way, adjacent to said Blocks 1 and 2, and all of May Place 
(60’ R/W), adjacent to said Blocks 1 and 2, McCann Homesites, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the  southwest corner of the intersection of E. Pershing Boulevard and N. College 
Drive).  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt with staff condition #1, seconded by Mr. Luna .  Eric Hammer, 
Planning and Development office, explained , upon inquiry, there are currently no confirmed tenants for 
the area at this time and there may be a future p lat action.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members 
of the governing body. (#5880) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Mission 
Village, situated in a portion of the S½SE¼ of Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th 
P.M., City of Cheyenne, L aramie County, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to Dell Range 
Boulevard, east of Griffith Avenue).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development office, stated discussion had 
been held regarding access onto Dell Range and the applicant has submitted a revised plat map to reduce 
the size of the access easement on the south portion of the lot.  Mr. Lloyd submitted a copy of the revised 
map (on file with City Clerk’s office).  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to amend by revised Final Plat map dated September 23, 2017, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to 
amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
-- all members of the governing body.  (#5881) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Joining the City of Cheyenne to a Community-Wide Visioning and Planning 
Initiative.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Dr. Joe Schaffer, President, 
Laramie County Community College, provided a brief background on the purpose of the initiative; 
proposed member monetary contribution, and his interpretation of the definition of the terms “rigorous 
assessment” and “common purpose”.  Dr. Schaffer noted there is no official title for the initia tive group 
as yet; explained engagement of a consultant is planned to provide evidence formed results to identify 
common community goals within a 12 month time frame, and stated grant funding through the Economic 
Development Joint Powers Board will assist with the project.  Discussion included past planning 
endeavors regarding studies with no follow -up implementation.  During discussion, Dr. Rinne raised a 
point of order regarding who had the floor to speak with Mayor Orr clarifying she would entertain 
continuing discussion by City Council members.  Discussion continued on determining role 
responsibilities involving property development or redevelopment and included additional information 
provided by Dr. Schaffer. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#5882) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming State Loan and 
Investment Board for a $1.6 Million Loan from the Clean W ater State Revolving Fund for funding the 
Dry Creek Interceptor Rehabilitation and auth orizing the Board Director to sign all loan d ocuments.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5883) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City’s Director of Community Recreation and Events, the City 
Treasurer and the City’s Events Coordinator to establish a process for expedited review and approval of 
contracts for events scheduled at City venues – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] State Real Property Lease Agreement No. 8 -09466 between the City of Cheyenne and State of 
Wyoming, Department of Administration and Information, General Services, for rental of parking spaces 
located at the Cox Parking Structure (revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement for CB&Q Rail Trail and Avenues Connector between City of 
Cheyenne and Western Research & Development Ltd. – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Rental Agreement renewal between City of Cheyenne Ice and Events Center and Cheyenne 
Capitals Youth Hockey Association (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the Wyoming Department of Health, 
Aging Division, to provide supportive services to persons 60 years or older under Title III-B Program 
Grant for FY18 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of Transportation for 
Wyoming Rural Public Transit funds to assist in providing local match for operating funds for the Transit 
program for FY2018 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-3-18 for furnishing two new 2017 four-wheel steering street sweepers for 
the Street & Alley Division (2015-2018 1% sales tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-4-18 for furnishing one new 2017 1500 chipper for the Forestry Division 
(2015-2018 1% sales tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract 6628 between the City of Cheyenne and Pole Mountain 
Electric, Inc. for the Dell Range/Yellowstone Signal Upgrade Project (2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $15,000.00). (#6628) 
 
[CA] Amendment to Contract No. 6630, Professional Services Agreement fo r Benefit Consulting 
Services between the City of Cheyenne and NOVO Benefits LLC (f/k/a Corp orate Benefits Consortium, 
LLC).  Denise Freeman, Director of Human Resources, provided a brief history on the employee health 
benefits program and explained the company’s consulting services that have been provided, since March, 
in reviewing and evaluating a new insurance provider, resulting in cost savings to the City, and providing 
employee advocate services.  She noted the amendment would continue services through December and 
will assist staff with professional expertise, including compliance with the Affordable Care Act and other 
federal regulations.  She indicated a Request for Proposals for future consultant services will be issued 
and stated she is looking at forming an employee benefits committee.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Freeman explained data is currently being gathered on loss 
ratio/history to be more proactive in seeking future insurance coverage proposals .  Discussion included 
timeliness of future insurance coverage proposals and training to staff that can be provided by the 
consultant.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Cook and Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#6630) 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Memorandum of Understanding (Contract No. 6680) between the City of 
Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Laramie County, Wyoming, to provide public transportation services to 
Laramie County Community College and for an area of Laramie County immediately adjacent to the City. 
(Revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6680) 
Re-Appointment of Maynard J. Richmeier to the Cheyenne Housing Authority Board.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointee). 
 Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held October 9, 2017 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Ave nue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance of 
annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exists for all of the W1/2 Section 14, all 
of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 21; Township 13 North, Range 66 Wes t of the 
6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and 
Avenue C). 
Under other business: 
 Mr. Johnson commented on business advertising sales practices by CGI Communications 
(company under contract with the City to produce an updated community video to be posted on the City 
website which includes local business sponsorships included in the video format), noting he has received 
several complaints  about the company’s assertive marketing practices.  Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, 
advised the contract has been reviewed; that local businesses may decline to participate in advertising or 
they may contact the Mayor’ s office with their concerns, and that contact with CGI could be made to 
ameliorate the company’s local marketing approach. 
 
 Mr. Johnson requested an update on Mayor  Bloomberg’s Mayors Challenge grant funding 
initiative.  Mayor Orr advised City staff and select members of the community  recently met to discuss 
community issues that may be considered for submission for the initiative.  She  provided information on 
the issues and ideas that were reviewed, noting the Flight the Blight Project was selected by the committee 
as a project to submit for the Challenge initiative by the October 20th application deadline. 
 
 Mr. Luna expressed appreciation to Public Works Department staff for addressing the 17 th Street 
alley concerns resulting in closure of access to the area. 
 
 Mr. Shanor moved, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §16-4-405 (a)(ii) and (iii), to meet in executive session 
for the purpose of considering a personnel matter and proposed litigation to which the City may be a party, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Mayor Orr advised the motion was not debatable.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”.  Mayor Orr stated the 
motion to move into executive session for the purpose stated by Council President Shanor carried; the 
executive session would be held in the City Council Administrative Office and would not be open to the 
public.  In addition to members of the governing body, Mayor Orr announced the City Attorney and City 
Clerk would attend the executive session, and following the execu tive session further action by the 
governing body would not be taken, therefore, the regular meeting of the governing body would be 
officially adjourned following closure of the executive session.  The governing body vacated Council 
Chambers at 7:39 p.m. to meet in executive session. 
 
 The meeting was officially adjourned at 8:53 p.m. following closure of the executive session. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
              
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  October 6, 2017

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-10-09 | CLIP_ID: 623 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 623

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 9, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Case, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Johnson and Mr. Shanor , Agenda Item # 17 was removed from t he Consent Agenda.   Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve remaining Conse nt A genda items, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on September 25, 2017.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr.  Case.  
Inquiry was made regarding vouchers payable to:  Affordable Lawn Care and Maintenance, LLC  
(mowing/weeding nuisance abatements ); The Elemental Dog, LLC (K9 dog training services); 
HDR, Inc. (Prairie Ave./Frontier Mall intersection improvements (roundabout)), and Novo 
Benefits, LLC (health insurance consultation services).   Providing information available on the 
vouchers of interest were:  Chief Brian Kozak , Police Department; Jim Voeller, City Engineer ; 
Ron Downey, City Treasurer; Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director, Community Recreation and Events 
Department, and Mayor Orr.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Under other business, Mr. Sanchez clarified the services provided by The Elemental Dog related 
to dog training for the Police Department. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a Public Hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exists for a portion of Section 9, all of the W½ 
Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 21; Township 13 North, 
Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to the southeast of the 
intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C).  Eric Hammer, Planning and Development office, 
provided information on the property requested by the landowner to be annexed , and reviewed 
requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.   Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Hammer confirmed the property owners decided to request annexation of all of the 
area that will be developed at this time.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of 
the public hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified

 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Interior 
Heights, Fourth Filing, a replat of all of Blocks 146, 147, 148 and 149, Inclusive, Interior Heights, 
Second Filing, City of Cheyenne,  Laramie County, Wyoming; along with all of those adjacent

of the governing body. (#5884) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City’s Director of Community Recreation and Events, 
the City Treasurer and the City’s Events Coordinator to establish a process for expedited review 
and approval of contracts for events scheduled at City venues.  David Soules, Events Coordinator, 
Community Recreation and Events Department, provided information on the purpose of the 
resolution, noting the process will expedite procedures involving contracts for performance acts at 
the Civic Center, Ice and Events Center and other City-owned venues.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Soules explained a template contract document has 
been prepared and reviewed by the City Attorney which will result in a more efficient and quicker 
turn-around time for booking acts for performances; summarized past procedures and the need for 
timely contract offerings , and commented on the department’s goal of booking more acts at the 
Civic Center.  Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, provided additional information on past pr actices 
involving contracts for acts  at the Civic Center ; purpose of the new process ; review by the City 
Attorney and City Treasurer for any contract acts requesting deviation from the template format, 
and quarterly reports , including type of content,  that will be provided to the governing body 
pursuant to the resolution.  Discussion included how the expedited process would be structured; 
oversight by City Attorney and City Treasurer; timing of reports; proof of insurance requirements, 
and mechanism used to determine ticket prices to recognize a break-even point. (#5885) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Swan Ranch Rail Park, Eleventh Filing Subdivision, a replat of Lots 1 through 10, Block 1 and 
Lots 1 through 17, Block 2 of the Swan Ranch Rail Park Eighth Filing and being situated in 
portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23 and 27, Township 13 north, Range 67 west of the 6th Principal 
Meridian, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of Tundra Drive, south of  
W. Wallick Road and west of Clear Creek Pa rkway) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for development of a new 
office building to be served by an on -site septic system located generally within Township 14, 
Range 66, Section 25, Laramie County, Wyoming (south of Dell Range Blvd. at its intersection 
with US 30) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] Preliminary Plat for Whitney Ranch, 1st Filing, a replat of all of Tracts 31 and 32 and that 
portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition, situate in 
Section 23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard) – referred to 
Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] Conditional Approval of a Concept Preliminary Plat for the Sweetgrass Subdivision, 
consisting of unplatted lands being all of the SE ¼ SE ¼ Section 10, S ½ S ½ W ½ Section 11, S 
½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 21; Township 13 North, 
Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming  (located south of College Drive and 
east of Avenue C) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] State Real Property Lease Agreement No. 8-09466 between the City of Cheyenne and State 
of Wyoming, Department of Administration and Information, General Services, for rental of 
parking spaces located at the Cox Parking Structure (revenue to the City) .  Motion (p er Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6732)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement for CB&Q Rail Trail and Avenues Connector between 
City of Cheyenne and Western Research & Development Ltd.   Motion (p er Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6733) 
 
[CA] Rental Agreement renewal between City of Cheyenne Ice and Events Center and Cheyenne 
Capitals Youth Hockey Association (revenue to City) .  Motion (per Conse nt Agenda) carried.         
(#6734) 
 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the Wyoming Department 
of Health, Aging Division, to provide supportive services to persons 60 years or older under Title 
III-B Program Grant for FY18.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6735)  
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of Transportation 
for Wyoming Rural Public Transit funds to assist in providing local match for operating funds for 
the Transit program for FY2018.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6736)  
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications 
Cable Franchise renewal an d CenturyLink Cable Television Franchise agreements with River 
Oaks Communications Corporation – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -4-18 for furnishing two new 2017 four -wheel steering street 
sweepers for the Street & Alley Division (2015 -2018 1% S ales Tax).  Motion (per  Consent 
Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $443,134.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-3-18 for furnishing one new  2017 1500 chipper for the Forestry 
Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$40,675.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-2-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Reim an Corp., Cheyenne, WY, for the Pershing and Duff Crosswalk Project 
(2015-2018 1% Sales Tax)  – referred to Finance Committee.   Under other business and upon 
inquiry, Mayor Orr confirmed the item was referred to Finance Committee and noted the project 
design has been completed. 
 
Appointment of Bruce Heimbuck and Del Lummis to the Downtown Development 
Authority Board.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded  by Mr. Roybal.  Upon request, Mayor 
Orr provided information on Mr. Heimbuck’s professional background involving knowledge of 
DDA/Main Street programs.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointees). 
 
[CA] Satellite Manufacturing Permit application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Chronicles 
Distilling, LLC, d/b/a Chronicles Distilling, 1506 T homes Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Finance Committee.  
 In addition to comments made  under other business  previously referenced above, the 
following topics were raised: 
 
Upon inquiry regarding discontinuation of the annual City sponsored Halloween Goblin 
Walk, Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director, Community Recreation and Events Department, explained 
there are two new events planned for this year in lieu of the Goblin Walk which will be more cost 
effective, and review of the new events will be conducted to determine how successful they were.   
  
Mr. Case commented for the record on an opinion piece that was recently published in the 
Washington Examiner about FEMA funds the City is receiving for the Civic Center Commons 
Project relative to FEMA funding needed for hurricane relief efforts in Puerto Rico, and how it 
painted the City in a negative light .  Upon inquiry regarding evacuation shelters available in the 
community in the event of a disaster, Mayor Orr explained a disaster recovery plan is in the process 
of being finalized through the City’s membership in the Wyoming Association of Risk 
Management, and the plan will be shared with Council members when it is completed. 
 
Also under other business and pursuant to inquiry:  Mayor Orr advised she would 
communicate with Council members when her schedul e includes out of town absences; Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, provided information on the status of 3 “parked” liquor licenses pursuant 
to state statute changes that became effective July 1 st, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised 
she would forward monthly reports she receives from the Animal Shelter to the governing body. 
  
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:52 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk 
   
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

  October 23, 2017
