MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-10-23 | CLIP_ID: 625 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 625

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 23, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne (arriving at 6:07 p.m.), Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie 
Soden, Deputy City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Roybal, Agenda Item # 20(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve remaining Conse nt Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna .  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on October 9, 2017.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Roybal declared a conflict (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy Dodge, 
Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.   Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouche rs as 
presented, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Upon inquiry, Ron Downey, City Treasurer , provided 
information available on vouchers payable to the following: Aircycle Corp. (machine for 
destroying and removing mercury from florescent light bulbs); All Traffic Data Services, Inc. 
(traffic counts survey for MPO); AVI Engineering (20th & Duff Crosswalk professional services); 
E/S3 Consultants, Inc. (ene rgy efficiency study at Ice & Events Center), and  Green House Data 
(discovery and recommendations for business continuity for disaster recovery).  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” -- all membe rs of the governing body  present in Council Chambers .  Following 
announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Chambers.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Section 9, all of the W½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 
21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to 
the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C).  Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading with staff conditions #1 through 5, 
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from MR Medium -Density Residential to 
CB Comm unity Business for Lot 4, Block 2, Moore -Haven Heights Addition, 3rd Filing, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of 8th Avenue west of Central Avenue).  Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from HR High-Density Residential to CB 
Community Business for Lot 3, Lot 4, and the W 22' of Lot 2, Block 745, Stanfield Addition; and 
Lots 1, 2, & 5, Block 1, Stanfield Addition, 3rd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west of the 
intersection of Deming Dr ive and Walterscheid Boulevard ).  Mr. Case moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development  
Department, provided information on  a proposed Greenway path in the area, noting it will not  
affect the parcel.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Vacating the eastern one-half of the 16-foot wide alley 
located west of House Avenue, south of Evans Avenue, adjacent to Lots 1, 2, 3, 12, 13 and 14 of 
Block 2, Pershing Heights Addition, Second Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  
Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna .  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from Swan Ranch Rail 
Park Planned Unit De velopment to Light Industrial for a portion of Lots 11, 12, 13, 14 and 15, 
Block 2 of the Swan Ranch Rail Park Eleventh Filing Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located 
north of Tundra Drive, south of  W. Wallick Road a nd west of Clear Creek Parkway).  Mr. C ase 
moved to approve on second reading  with staff condition #1 , seconded by Mr.  White.  Upon 
inquiry, Eric Hammer, Planning and Development  Department, confirmed that condition #1 
rectifies the possibility of the land having two different zoning designations.   Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 8.40, Food and 
Food Service Establishments, Title 8, Health and Safety, of the code o f the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Johnson and Mr. White) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Swan Ranch Rail Park, Eleventh Filing Subdivision, a replat of Lo ts 1 through  10, Block 1 and 
Lots 1 through 17, Block 2 of the Swan Ranch Rail Park Eighth Filing and being situated in 
portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23 and 27, Township 13 north, Range 67 west of the 6th Principal 
Meridian, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of Tundra Drive, south of  
W. Wallick Road and west of Clear Creek Pa rkway).  Mr. Case moved to adopt  with Planning 
Commission conditions #1 and 2, seconded by Mr. C ook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#5886) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for development of a new 
office building to be served by an on -site septic system located generally within Township 14, 
Range 66, Section 25, Laramie County, Wyoming (s outh of Dell Range Blvd. at its intersection 
with US 30).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5887) 
 
 [CA] RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2017 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16 -4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying compliance with W.S. §15 -1-402 for the City -initiated 
annexation of a portion of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, 
R67W, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Old Happy Jack Road and Happy Jack Road, 
west of I-80) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 Preliminary Plat for Whitney Ranch, 1st Filing, a replat of all of Tracts 31 and 32 and that 
portion of James Drive intervening; and a portion of Tract 39, Hill Heights Addition, situate in 
Section 23, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, 6th P.M., County of Laramie, State of Wyoming 
(located east of and adjacent to Van Buren Avenue, north of Dell Range Boulevard).  Mr. Case 
moved to acknowledge the Planning Commission’s recommendation to approve with staff 
condition #1, seconded by Dr. Rinne. Upon inquiry, Er ic Hammer, Planning and Development  
Department, explained that staff condition #1 is related to the James Place right-of-way, noting the 
developer believes it should be 60 ft. wide at that location. He advised a traffic study will determine 
the correct width. Mr. Hammer also provided information on the proposed 2nd Filing for Whitney 
Ranch to the north, which  will be unusually shaped due to topography.   Brad Emmons, AVI 
Engineering, agent for the developer, explained the master plan is for about 600 acres and all areas 
are being reviewed concurrently, and stated arterial and collector roads are currently the only ones 
that have been laid out. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 Conditional Approval of a Concept Preliminary Plat for the Sweetgrass Subdivision, 
consisting of unplatted lands being all of the SE ¼ SE ¼ Section 10, S ½ S ½ W ½ Section 11, S 
½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 21; Township 13 North, 
Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming  (located south of College Drive and 
east of Avenue C).  Mr. Case moved to acknowledge the Planning Commission’s recommendation 
of conditional approval with cond itions #1 and 2, seconded by Dr. Rinne.   Mr. Case moved to 
amend by revised Concept Preliminary Plat dated October 20, 2017 which satisfies condition #2, 
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -  all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications 
Cable Franchise renewal an d CenturyLink Cable Television Franchise agreements with River 
Oaks Communications Corporation.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$51,635.00).  (#6743) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and 
the United States Secret Service for Colorado and Wyom ing Financial Crimes Task Force – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Grant Award Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, and 
State of Wyoming, Office of Homeland Security, for the purchase of one trailer mounted mobile 
surveillance platform and allowable accessories – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Employment Agreement between the C ity of Cheyenne, through the Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Jessica Fritz for c rime victim assistance services – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, through the Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Leslie Bur ch for c rime victim assistance services – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, through the Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Brenda Johnson for c rime victim assistance services – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI to develop the 
Evers Blvd. Reconstruction design – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Downtown 
Development Authority to establish the responsibilities of how and processes by which the Fiscal 
Year 2017-2018 funds are to be used in the Cheyenne central business district – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of  Cheyenne and Collins Engineers, 
Inc., for Christensen Road constructability review services – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Amendment No. 2 to Contract No. 6162 between the City of Cheyenne, through the 
Information Technology Division, and Century Link Sales Solution, Inc. for modification to the 
City’s Metro Ethernet Network servicing outlying offices – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #S-2-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Reiman  Corp., Cheyenne, WY, for the Per shing and Duff Crosswalk Project  
(2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to accept the bid 
from Reiman Corp., Cheyenne, WY, in an a mount not to exceed $76,300.50, seconded by Mr.  
White.  Discussion followed and included: project importance; Mayor’s Council for People with 
Disabilities’ involvement with the project; traffic increases overall throughout the City and  
construction time frame for similar projects.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  (#6744) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -7-18 for furnishing ice control product for the Public Works 
Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-8-18 for furnishing one in-stock fire apparatus for the Cheyenne 
Fire and Rescue Department – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Satellite Manufacturing Permit application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Chronicles 
Distilling, LLC, d/b/a Chronicles Distilling, 1506 T homes Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Mr. 
Luna moved to approve contingent upon City inspections and submittal of proof of the Wyoming 
Liquor Division’s manufacturing license, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Aaron Lesher, 
co-applicant, explained the business venture is fully funded through himself and his brother, and 
that distribution of their product will mainly be done through the Wyoming Liquor Division.  Mr. 
Lesher stated that they are working with City and state entities to be come fully compliant with 
requirements for the establishment, and welcomed Council members to contact them at any time 
with any questions.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 Announcement was made of the appointments of Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, McCoy Bush, 
Ashen Doty, Raegan Downham, Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Keiran Loughran, Emily Lucero, 
Nickolas Metheny, Kaylee Sanchez, Kenitra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Garrett Vickery to 
the 2017-18 Mayor’s Youth Council (to be confirmed November 13, 2017).  
 Under other business: 
 
Rev. Rick Veit and Ed Boenish, representing Compassionate Cheyenne, explained  the 
vision and mission of the program and how it began, stating that the general goal is to nurture the 
growth of compassion in the community.  Rev. Veit and Mr. Boenish advised there is an 
international Compassion Charter which will involve a future resolution to have Cheyenne become 
a compassionate City.  It was noted there will be a public community forum on compassion at the 
library.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Boenish stated there is no correlation between being a sanctuary City 
and a compassionate City.  Discussion followed and included local and national goals a nd 
sanctuary Cities/states.  Upon inquiry as to whether the upcoming resolution rela ted to 
Compassionate Cheyenne will be impacted by state mandates on sanctuary rules , Sylvia Hackl, 
City Attorney, expressed her belief that supporting the resolution will not countermand any rules 
or anything otherwise set forth by the state. 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on the dollar amount of a  voucher payable to Affordable Lawn 
Care & Maintenance LLC that was presented for paymen t at the Council meeting on October 9, 
2017 for nuisance abatement services for 7 properties. 
 
Mr. Roybal spoke in reference to a project he is interested in involving repairing the clocks 
on the Spiker parking garage. He stated he would like to move forward with receiving a price on 
the cost for the project with Mayor Orr advising her office will assist in obtaining quotes. 
 
Mr. Case advised he has received several concerns regarding an intersection at Cheshire 
Drive and Foxcroft Drive, and he would like research conducted for a solution for traffic issues in 
the area. 
 
Mr. Case moved, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §16 -4-405 (a)(ii) and (iii), to meet in executive 
session for the purpose of receiving a legal briefing from the City Attorney on a matter concerning 
litigation to which the governing body may be a party or proposed litigation to which the governing 
body may be a party, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mayor Orr advised the motion was not debatable.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor Orr stated the motion 
to move into executive session for the purpose stated by Councilman Case carried; the executive 
session would be held in the City Council Administrative Office and would not be open to the 
public.  In addition to members o f the governing body, Mayor Orr announced the City Attorney , 
Assistant City Attorney (Ms. McCoy) and Deputy City Clerk would attend the executive session, 
and following the executive session further action by the governing body would not be taken, 
therefore, the regular meeting of the governing body would be officially adjourned following 
closure of the executive session.  The governing body vacated Council Chambers at 6:46 p.m. to 
meet in executive session. 
  
The meeting was officially adjourned at 7:28 p.m. following closure of the executive 
session. 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kylie Soden 
        Deputy City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 13, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.   Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne and Dicky Shanor. Absent: Jeff White .  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, 
City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Johnson, Agenda Item #21(g) (Memorandum of Understanding between the 
City and Downtown Development Authority) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  In addition, 
Mr. Laybourn requested to remove agenda item #21(g) from the Council agenda in its entirety.

item was removed from the agenda.  R emaining Consent Agenda items were approved.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.

moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”

governing body present. 
 
 ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 1.12.010, Findings of fact, and

according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16 -4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#5888) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying compliance with W.S. §15 -1-402 for the City -initiated

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and

[CA] Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, through the Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Brenda Johnson for c rime victim assistance services . Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (base annual salary amount of $35,859.12 plus eligible benefits). (#6754) 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI to develop the 
Evers Blvd. Reconstruction design .  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed  
$776,266.37, seconded by Mr Cook .  Comment was made on the project’s street and drainage 
improvements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with 
the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting "no". (#6755) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne a nd the Downtown 
Development Authority to establish the responsibilities of how and processes by which the Fiscal 
Year 2017-2018 funds are to be used in the Cheyenne central business district. Item was withdrawn 
from the agenda pursuant to action taken during the Consent Agenda item. 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Collins Engineers, 
Inc., for Christensen Road cons tructability review services.  Donn Edmunds spoke in support of 
the original project’s scope of work in conjunc tion with discussions with WYDOT, and in 
opposition to the current proposed scope of work.  Mr. Edmunds discussed the differences between 
the initial and current proposals , and expressed concerns with traffic congestion; emergency 
response, and lack of adequate 6th penny sales tax funding.  Mr. Luna reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount 
not to exceed $312,235.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Case moved to postpone to November 27, 
2017, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  It was noted postponement will allow for more information to be 
received on the project during a City Council work session to be held Friday, November 17 th at 
noon, which is open to the public.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
[CA] Contract for Services between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and the 
Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board for the weekender community 
service program (funding through Joint Powers Board) – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, 
and State of Wyoming, Office of Homeland Security, for the purposes of administering Regional 
Emergency Response Team #7 (RERT #7) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Amendment No. 2 to Contract No. 6162 between the City of Cheyenne, through the 
Information Technology Division, and CenturyLink Sales Solution, Inc. for modification to the 
City’s Metro Ethernet Network servicing outlying offices.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  
(#6162) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 8 to Contract No. 6219 between the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation and Events Department, and RECCO Inc., d/b/a Five-R Company, for the 
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Project, to increa se the Guaranteed Maximum Price – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 6 to Contract No. 4563 between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Wyoming Cattle Co., LLC, for grazing on the Belvoir Ranch (revenue to City) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6 654 between the City of Cheyenne, 1% 
Construction Division, and Simon Contractors, to extend the completion date for the 20 17 City 
Overlay, Part 1 Project – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #E -7-18 for furnishing ice control product fo r the Public Works 
Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Luna moved to accept the bid from Desert Mountain, 
Riverton, WY, in an amount not to exceed  $125,370.00, seconded by Mr. Johnson .  Comment 
followed on evaluating other types of ice control products which have less effect on concrete and 
vehicles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Laybourn voting "no". 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-8-18 for furnishing one in-stock fire apparatus for the Cheyenne 
Fire and Rescue Department .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept ed bid submitted by 
Max Fire Apparatus, Castle Rock, CO, in an amount not to exceed $478,000.00).  
 
[CA] Consideration of B id #S-3-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, and Wasatch Railroad Contractors for the restoration of Big 
Boy 4004 Holiday Park Project (State Loan and Investment Board grant funds ) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 Appointment of Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, McCoy Bush, Ashen Doty, Raegan Downham, 
Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Keiran Loughran, Emily Lucero, Nickolas Metheny, Kaylee Sanchez, 
Kenitra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Garrett Vickery to the 2017-18 Mayor’s Youth Council.  
Mr. Shanor moved to approve, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(Mayoral appointments). 
 Appointment of Jasper Ja mes Chen to the Cheyenne/Laramie County Economic 
Development Joint Powers Board.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Shanor.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Johnson commented on u pcoming employee retirements in the Street and Alley 
Division and potentially utilizing salary savings toward salary adjustments to other employees 
within the Division, and questioned the amount of property taxes the City pays for park properties.  
Ron Downey, City Treasurer, explained that if the land is owned by the City and used for a 
government purpose, which includes parks, it is not taxable.  Mayor Orr noted there are some City-
owned properties for which the City does pay property taxes, such as the Belvoir Ranch. 
 
 Mayor Orr called for a moment of silence in remembrance of  colleague Rob Cleveland , 
Director of the City of Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, who recently 
passed away, and noted funeral services will be held Saturday, November 18th at 2:00 p.m. in the 
Civic Center.  
 
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:35 p.m. 
 
        Submitted by, 
     
     
Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2017-11-27 | CLIP_ID: 629 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 629

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 27, 2017 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.   Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Ca se, Bryan Cook,  Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott 
Roybal, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Mayor Orr announced that agenda item #18 (Resolution) was being removed at the request 
of the sponsors. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. Roybal and Mr. Johnson , Agenda Item #20(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of  the Governing Body on November 13, 2017.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mr. Luna moved to approve payment  of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  
Inquiry was made regarding vouchers payable to: Gregory Hoggatt (employee moving expenses); 
Affordable Lawn & Tree Care (nuisance property abatements), Brown Shoe Fit (employee safety 
boots); Burke Moving & Storage (Metropolitan Planning Organization’s office moving expenses); 
Cheyenne Regional Medical Center (sexual assault nurse exams); Cheyenne Fence Company 
(accident involving fence damage); Clean Harbors Environmental Services (household hazardous 
waste disposal fees); Wyoming Business Council (payment associated with grant for Swan Ranch), 
and Service Pro of Cheyenne  (water damage clean -up).  Providing information available on the 
vouchers of interest were:  Mayor Orr; Ron Downey, City Treasurer; Vicki Nemecek, Director of 
Public Works; Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Section 9, all of the W½ Section 14, all of Section 15, a portion of Section 16, and all of Section 
21; Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located to  
the southeast of the intersection of E. College Drive and Avenue C) (postponed from 11/13/2017).  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to December 11, 2017 at the request of 
the developer, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 8.40, Food and 
Food Service Establishments, Title 8, Health and Safety, of the code o f the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4211) 
 
 ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 1.12.010, Findings of fact, and 
amending Section 1.12.020, Filing of plats and maps, of Chapter 1.12, Datum Plane, of Title 1, 
General Provisions, of the code of the city of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending various sections of Title 10, Vehicles  and 
Traffic, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming  – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Swan Ranch Rail Park, Twelfth Filing, a plat of land being situated in portions of the NE¼ NE¼ 
of Section 28, and the NW¼, NW¼ of Section 27, T13N, R67W of the 6 th Principal Meridian, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located to the north of the intersection of Berwick Drive and Tundra 
Drive).  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt with staff conditions #1 & 2, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5890) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Swan Ranch Rail Park, Thirteenth Filing, a plat of land being situated in a portion of Section 22, 
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the Sixth Principal Meridian, Laram ie County, Wyoming 
(located to the west of and adjacent to Berwick D rive, south of W. Wallick Road).  Mr. Roybal 
moved to adopt with staff condition #1, seconded by Mr . Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. (#5891) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Adopting the Converse Dell Range Intersection Traffic Safety Plan and 
the Converse Avenue 35% Design Plan.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Case.  
Appreciation was expressed to M etropolitan Planning Office (MPO) staff  for providing driving 
scenario information.  Comment was made on following through with various project plans acted 
upon by the governing body; acknowledging receipt vs. adopting the plans, and accountability of 
the governing body relative to approving plans.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by striking the word 
“adopting” and inserting “ acknowledging receipt ” of the plans and making conforming 
amendments through out the rest of the text , seconded by Mr. Johnson.   Tom Mason, MPO  
Director, explained the different levels of action (i.e. approving, adopting and acknowledging 
receipt) that can be taken on plans and encouraged Council to adopt the plans to provide staff with 
guidance to move forward with finding any funding opportunities for the project.  Discussion 
followed on previous plans, such as the Downtown Parking Study, in which the governing body 
acknowledged receipt of the study vs . adoption; inactivity or non -implementation of plans, and 
governing body’s potential accountability for plan contents.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City 
Clerk, stated it was her recollection that the Downtown Parking Study resolution was 
acknowledged by receipt, and noted some recommendations of the study are in process .  
Discussion continued on the definition and implication of “acknowledging receipt” vs. “adopting”.  
Motion to amend carried .  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5892) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming’s application for a Land and 
Water Conservation Fund Grant for dugout roof replacement and concrete and masonry block 
repair at the Cheyenne Junior League Complex.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5893) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing a one time payment to full -time and permanent part -time 
employees of the City of Cheyenne (excluding the Firefighters Union Local No. 279 and 
employees of the Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities) in recognition of their outstandi ng efforts.  
Stephanie Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association  (CPEA), thanked the 
governing body for supporting the resolution.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by increasing the one-time payment 
from 10% to 14% of the overage funds from the 2017 Fiscal Year budget, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Discussion included:  A ddressing City employee pay inequities;  prior employee bonuses;  
employee incentives and rewards related to controlling budget expenditures; future policy 
regarding employee bonuses and retaining and focusing on the initial 10% allocation; lack of prior 
communication by CPEA regarding its  recommendation of 14% that was proposed and 
recommended for approval at the Finance Committee; employee criteria  in receiving the bonus; 
employee increased out -of-pocket expenses due to new  health insurance provider, and City vs . 
employee FICA tax burden expense .  During discussion and u pon inquiry, Ron Downey , City 
Treasurer, explained the  employee inclusion requirements to be eligible for the bonus  and 
reviewed four options involving the percentage bonus amount (10% vs. 14%) relative to gross/net 
amounts, including tax expense amounts; Mayor Orr verified that Fire Union employees would 
not be included in the bonus payment  and advised the proposed 14% increase would assist with 
holding employees harmless relative to the required tax deduction , and Sylvia Hackl, City 
Attorney, and Fire Chief Greg Hoggatt provided information on terms of the current Fire Union 
collective labor agreement.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr.  Johnson, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor 
and Mr. White.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#5894) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts in the 
amount of $58,535.43 pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §16 -4-502 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing a one-time payment to each member of the bargaining unit 
of Cheyenne Firefighters Local Union Number 279 in settlement of the grievance filed by the 
Union against the City concerning health insurance benefits – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Shanor) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Declaring opposition to the elimination or reduction of the Federal 
Historic Preservation Tax Incentives Program (Sponsors - Mr. Roybal and Mr. White).  Resolution 
was withdrawn from the agenda at the request of the sponsors e arlier in the meeting.  [Refer to 
other business for additional information] 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Collins Engineers, 
Inc., for Christensen Road cons tructability review services  (postponed from 11/13/2017).   Ben 
Rowland, City Attorney’s office, spoke on the purpose of the agreement generally  and reviewed 
proposed changes and cost saving alternatives to certain Exhibit pages related to the agreement.  
He explained options for approval of additional services included in proposed Exhibit changes , 
and the proposed revision to the compensation schedule .  Tom Mason, Director, Metropolitan 
Planning Office (MPO), questioned a modification change contained in the alternatives relative to 
the Pershing and Christensen intersection being a T-design vs. 4 legged intersection.  John Volk 
reviewed committee representation, which included himself, and background of the committee’s 
and City staff review of plans submitted in response to the two Requests for Proposals issued for 
the project. He discussed plans submitted by Benchmark that were unsigned and unstamped; need 
for a construction management firm’s involvement and support for the contra ct with Collins 
Engineers with the alternatives; length of time for design plans to be completed; design costs, and 
other Benchmark projects involving City projects .  Jennifer Merrill, speaking on behalf of four 
property owners located at the end of the overpass near the bridge, discussed Benchmark’s plans, 
which they communicated to the homeowners,  to utilize Tate Road as the ir point of access.  She 
explained the types of businesses herself and the other property owners have and the need for 
sufficient and safe road width access  involving various equipment types ; advised of an alternate 
option they proposed involving land donation to provide for access further down the road , with 
road maintenance by the homeowners as the county will not be maintaining; cost savings that could 
be realized by their proposal, and timeliness of awarding bids to begin the project relative to cost.  
Mr. Rowland clarified that wording contained in Exhibit A -8, under basic services, encompassed 
the design changes alluded to by Ms.  Merrill.  Mr. Luna reported there was no recommendation 
on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve, seconded by Mr . Roybal.  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend, seconded by Mr. Case, by deleting the wording contained in item #6 
on page Exhibi t A-8 and inserting the wording as a new item #7 on page Exhibit A -9; amend 
wording on page Exhibit A -9 (last paragraph of subsection A -5, A.5.1, 5.1.1) to read “The 
Additional Services shall be performed only if authorized in writing by the City prior to their 
performance.  Consultant shall start activity only upon written approval of the City.  In order to 
provide adequate separation of responsibilities such that no one person controls all phases of a 
transaction involving expenditure of additional funds, additional signatures of the Mayor, the City 
Council President or his or her designee, and the City Engineering Department’s project manager 
will be required for approval of each Additional Service requested by the City.” and amend Exhibit 
B and C per red -lined conforming amendments (as previously explained by Mr. Rowland and 
included in the proposed substitute documentation) with regard to  changes to the Compensation 
Schedule and Project Schedule.  Explanation was provided on the intent of the changes involving 
adjustment to the UPRR permit process item (new #7 on page Exhibit A -9), guaranteed vs . not 
guaranteed contract value in the Compen sation Schedule, and extension of time frame in the 
Project Schedule.  Upon inquiry, Jim Voeller, City Engineer,  and Mr. Rowland confirmed they 
had reviewed and had no objections to the proposed amendments.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, 
City Attorney, provided information on the meaning of stamped plans and an upcoming meeting 
with Benchmark.  Mr. Case commented for the record that it was his understanding that under the 
project descript ion on page Exhibit A-1, item #4, the project as it will go out to bid , and the 
drawings, will include a modern roundabout at E. Pershing and Christensen, and noted to go 
outside that scope would require approval by the mayor, a Council member and engineering staff.  
Mayor Orr agreed with Mr. Case’s statement.  Mr. Rowland advised that steady progress has been 
made with Benchmark.  Upon inquiry, Mark Escobedo, City Engineer ’s office, confirmed he is 
satisfied with the options provided and he didn’t believe anything would need to be added  to the 
bid and that flexibility for bid alternate options would be available based upon funding.  Comments 
followed in support of the agreement and moving forward with the project ; roundabout design; 
access proposed by the property owners off Tate Road, and issuing the bid soon.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry regarding Mr. Mason’s 
question regarding a T -intersection vs. a roundabout, Mayor Orr advised Exhibit A -1, item #4, 
which references a modern roundabout, answered the question.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” –  all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. 
(#6759) 
 
[CA] Contract for Services between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and the 
Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board for the weekender community 
service program (fund ing through Joint Powers Board).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.    
(#6760) 
 
[CA] Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, 
and State of Wyoming, Office of Homeland Security, for the purposes of administering Regional 
Emergency Response Team #7 (RERT #7).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6761) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement for Municipal Finance  Counsel Services between the 
City of Cheyenne and Kutak Rock, LLP – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification No. 8 to Contract No. 6219 between the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation and Events Department, and RECCO Inc., d/b/a Five-R Company, for the 
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Project, to increase the Guaranteed Maximum Price.  Mr. Luna moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, Jason Sanchez, Community Recreation and 
Events, explained that modification costs were being covered by fundraising efforts of the Friends 
of the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Foundation.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#6219) 
 
[CA] Amendment No. 6 to Contract No. 4563 between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Wyoming Cattle Co., LLC, for grazing on the Belvoir Ranch (revenue to City).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#4563) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6654 between the City of Cheyenne, 1% 
Construction Division, and Simon Contractors, to extend the completion date for the 20 17 City 
Overlay, Part 1 Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6654) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -3-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, and Wasatch Railroad Contractors for the restoration of Big 
Boy 4004 Holiday Park Project (State Loan and Investment Board grant funds ).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $355,337.50). (#6762) 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Frank Cole expressed concern with retaining Converse Avenue as a two lane road relative 
to the Converse Dell Range Intersection Traffic Safety Plan acted upon during the meeting, noting 
traffic volumes and encouraging further review prior to any future bidding for the project.   
 
 Jeff Dierksen, discussed traffic concerns on Pinto Lane , specifically traffic volum e and 
speed limits in the area; referenced a petition he submitted a year ago, and requested action from 
the governing body in mitigating concerns .  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, provided 
information on a proposed written response she was preparing to Mr. Dierksen, and c orridor and 
traffic studies that had been conducted , which doesn’t warrant any change in reducing the speed 
limit.  Comments were made on responding to Mr. Dierksen’s concerns and the petition; traffic 
planning; due diligence and studies that have been conducted; incr ease in speed limits in certain 
neighborhoods pursuant to Engineering review and state law; correlating problems with utilization 
of speed strips and bumps, and traffic and speed related issues in other residential neighborhoods. 
 
 Mr. Johnson stated he had been contacted by constituents as to why there are two police 
officers present during traffic stops.  In response, Police Chief Brian Kozak explained usually there 
is only one officer on routine stops but that often times a second officer is requested to assist or is 
in the area and stops to offer assistance. 
 
 Mr. White provided information on agenda item #18(a) (Resolution declaring opposition 
to the elimination or reduction of the Federal Historic Preservation Tax Incentives Program) and 
the reason for requesting the resolution be withdrawn from the agenda, which is due to timeliness 
of action anticipated on the proposed tax reform bill at the national level which may affect program 
funding.  He commented on past program benefits available to developers  investing in historic 
downtown properties, and expressed reduction of program funding will increase obstacles related 
to revitalizing downtown.  He encouraged those who have concerns to contact their Wyoming 
congressional delegation. 
  
 Mr. Case expressed appreciation to Public Works for finding an innovative and inexpensive 
solution to a curb cut issue at the corner Continental Drive and Meadow Drive. 
   
 There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeti ng was 
officially adjourned at 7:52 p.m. 
 
 
        Submitted by, 
 
             
        Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
        City Clerk
